c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}\colon X_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{i-1}\otimes(Y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes Y_{s})\otimes X_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{p}\longrightarrow X

by the formula

(3.28)

(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})(x_{1},\dots,x_{i-1},y_{1},\dots,y_{s},x_{i+1},\dots,x_{p})\coloneqq\\
(-1)^{\maltese_{i}}c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{i-1},c_{2}(y_{1},\dots,y_{s}),x_{i+1},\dots,x_{p}),

with the sign given by

\displaystyle\maltese_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textstyle(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-1)(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-i)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-1+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}|x_{j}|\right)

\displaystyle=\textstyle(s-1)(p-i)+t\left(p-1+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}|x_{j}|\right),

If
X_{i}\neq Y
we set
c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}\coloneqq 0
.

3.29
.

Infinitesimal composition is associative in the sense that

(3.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{1}\bullet_{i}(c_{2}\bullet_{j}c_{3})&=(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})\bullet_{i+j-1}c_{3}\\
(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})\bullet_{j}c_{3}&=(-1)^{\maltese}(c_{1}\bullet_{j}c_{3})\bullet_{i+n-1}c_{2},\qquad j<i,\ n={\lvert c_{3}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}},\end{split}

whenever these are defined, where the sign is given by the following integer
modulo
2

\maltese=(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+1-\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}})(\lvert c_{3}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+1-\lvert c_{3}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}})=|\mathbf{s}c_{2}||\mathbf{s}c_{3}|.

3.31
.

The previous formula is motivated by the definition of the infinitesimal
compositions in the linear endomorphism operad of a pair of vector spaces
introduced in
[
BM09
]
. The extra signs—those not given by the Koszul
sign rule—stem from the implicit application of the so-called operadic
suspension, compare with
[
Mur16
, Definition 2.4]
.

3.32
.

Following
[
GV95
]
, we define

(3.33)

{c_{1}}\left\{c_{2}\right\}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{p}c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}.

3.34
.

Notice that the number of inputs of
{c_{1}}\left\{c_{2}\right\}
is
p+s-1
, as
expected from
Definition

3.20
.

Suppose given a further homogeneous operation

\displaystyle m\colon W_{1}\otimes W_{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W,

\displaystyle\lvert m\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2,

\displaystyle\lvert m\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle|m|\coloneqq 2.

With this additional datum we introduce the following notation.

3.35
.

For
c\coloneqq c_{1}
and
d(c)\coloneqq{m}\left\{c\right\}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\left\{m\right\}
. Explicitly,

(3.36)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d(c)(x_{1},\dots,x_{p+1})&=(-1)^{q|x_{1}|+q}m(x_{1},c(x_{2},\dots,x_{p+1}))\\
&\phantom{=}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{i+q}c(x_{1},\dots,m(x_{i},x_{i+1}),\dots,x_{p+1})\\
&\phantom{=}+(-1)^{p+1+q}m(c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),x_{p+1});\end{split}

compare with the definition of the differential in a brace algebra with
multiplication given in
3.23
.

3.37
.

We also define

(3.38)

{m}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}\coloneqq(m\bullet_{1}c_{1})\bullet_{p+1}c_{2}=(-1)^{|c_{1}||c_{2}|}(m\bullet_{2}c_{2})\bullet_{1}c_{1},

so that, if
W_{1}=X
and
W_{2}=Y
,

{m}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{q})\coloneqq(-1)^{\maltese}m(c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),c_{2}(y_{1},\dots,y_{q})),

with the sign given by

\displaystyle\maltese

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textstyle(|c_{1}|-1)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{i=1}^{\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}}|x_{i}|\right)

\displaystyle=\textstyle(p+q-1)+t\left(p+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|\right).

3.39
.

The number of inputs of
{m}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}
is
p+s
, as expected from

Definition

3.20
. Also,

(3.40)

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}|}{m}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}=(-1)^{t\left(p+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|\right)}m(c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),c_{2}(y_{1},\dots,y_{q})),

where
|\mathbf{s}c_{1}|\coloneqq|c_{1}|-1
, compare with the definition of the cup
product in a brace algebra with multiplication given in

3.23
.

3.6.
The Hochschild cochain complex

Let
A
be a graded algebra.

3.41
.

The
Hochschild cochain complex of
A
is the bigraded vector space

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
with the bihomogeneous components

(3.42)

\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)\coloneqq\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{q}\!\left(A^{\otimes p},A\right),\qquad p\geq 0,\ q\in\mathbb{Z},

As is customary, given a cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)
, we refer to the
horizontal degree
\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}=p
as the
Hochschild degree
and to the
vertical degree
\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}=q
as the
internal degree
.

3.43
.

We set

\displaystyle\operatorname{C}^{p}\!\left(A\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)=\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}\!\left(A^{\otimes p},A\right),\qquad p\geq 0.

The Hochschild cochain complex of
A
is a brace algebra (see

Remark

3.55
), and hence we may apply

3.23
to describe its algebraic structure.

Firstly, the bidegree
(-1,0)

pre-Lie product
is given by

(3.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}^{s,t}\!\left(A\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{p+s-1,q+t}\!\left(A\right)\\
c_{1}\otimes c_{2}\longmapsto{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}\coloneqq{c_{1}}\left\{c_{2}\right\},\end{split}

see
eq.

3.33
for the definition of
{c_{1}}\left\{c_{2}\right\}
. It follows
from
3.23
that
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a shifted graded Lie
algebra with the bidegree
(-1,0)

Gerstenhaber (Lie) bracket

(3.45)

[c_{1},c_{2}]\coloneqq{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}-(-1)^{(p+q-1)(s+t-1)}{c_{2}}\bullet{c_{1}},

with respect to the total degree. If
\chark{\mathbf{k}}=2
or
p+q
is even, we also
consider the
Gerstehaber square
operation

(3.46)

\Sq\colon\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{2p-1,2q}\!\left(A\right),\qquad c\longmapsto\Sq(c)\coloneqq{c}\bullet{c}.

The Hochschild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is also endowed with the multiplication

m_{2}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{2,0}\!\left(A\right)=\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{0}\!\left(A^{\otimes 2},A\right)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}\!\left(A^{\otimes 2},A\right)

given by the multiplication operation

m_{2}^{A}\colon A\otimes A\longrightarrow A,\qquad x\otimes y\longmapsto xy,

of the graded algebra
A
. Using that
m_{2}^{A}
has the bidegree
(2,0)
, the
associativity of the multiplication yields

\displaystyle({m_{2}^{A}}\bullet{m_{2}^{A}})(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

\displaystyle=-m_{2}^{A}(m_{2}^{A}(x_{1},x_{2}),x_{3})+m_{2}^{A}(x_{1},m_{2}^{A}(x_{2},x_{3}))=0,

so that
m_{2}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{2,0}\!\left(A\right)
is a brace algebra multiplication in the sense
of
3.23
. Consequently,
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is endowed with
the bidegree
(1,0)

Hochschild differential

(3.47)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}\colon\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{p+1,q}\!\left(A\right)\\
c&\longmapsto[m_{2}^{A},c]={m_{2}^{A}}\bullet{c}-(-1)^{p+q-1}{c}\bullet{m_{2}^{A}},\end{split}

and hence
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a differential bigraded vector space. Explicitly, using

eq.

3.36
we see that the action of the Hochschild differential on a
cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)
is given by the formula

(3.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}(c)(x_{1},\dots x_{p+1})&\coloneqq(-1)^{q|x_{1}|+q}x_{1}c(x_{2},\dots,x_{p+1})\\
&\phantom{=}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{i+q}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{i}x_{i+1},\dots,x_{p+1})\\
&\phantom{=}+(-1)^{p+1+q}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})x_{p+1}.\end{split}

3.49
.

The
Hochschild cohomology of
A
is the bigraded vector space

(3.50)

\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right),d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}\right).

3.51
.

We set

\displaystyle\operatorname{HH}^{p}\!\left(A\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{HH}^{p,q}\!\left(A,A\right),\qquad p\geq 0.

3.52
.

Endowed with the Hochschild differential,
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a shifted dg Lie algebra
with the Gerstenhaber bracket, and
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a shifted graded Lie algebra,
both with respect to the total degree.

Appealing again to
3.23
, the multiplication

m_{2}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
yields the bidegree
(0,0)

cup product

(3.53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}^{s,t}\!\left(A\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{p+s,q+t}\!\left(A\right)\\
c_{1}\otimes c_{2}&\longmapsto{c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}}\coloneqq(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}|}{m_{2}^{A}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\},\end{split}

so that
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is also a dg algebra and
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a Gerstehaber algebra with
the induced operations, both with respect to the total degree. Explicitly, we
see from
eq.

3.40
that

(3.54)

({c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s})\coloneqq\\
(-1)^{t(p+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|)}c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})c_{2}(x_{p+1},\dots,x_{p+s}).

3.55
.

The brace algebra structure on
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is obtained from the
shifted

endomorphism graded operad of
A
, which is isomorphic to the endomorphism
graded operad of the shifted graded vector space
A[1]
be means of a
non-trivial isomorphism, see for example
[
Mur16
, Remarks 2.5 and 2.7]
.

3.56
.

The Hochchild cochain complex is functorial under isomorphisms of graded
algebras: Given an isomorphism of graded algebras
\varphi\colon A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}B
, the isomorphism of graded vector spaces

\Phi_{p,q}\colon\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(B\right),\qquad c\longmapsto c^{\varphi},

where

c^{\varphi}(b_{1},\dots,b_{p})\coloneqq\varphi(c(\varphi^{-1}(b_{1})),\dots,\varphi^{-1}(b_{n})),

assemble into an isomorphism of differential bigraded vector spaces

\Phi\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(B\right)

that is strictly compatible with all of the algebraic structure described
above. In particular, the induced isomorphism of bigraded vector spaces

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\Phi\right)\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(B\right)

is an isomorphism of Gerstenhaber algebras with respect to the total degree.

3.57
.

The Hochschild cohomology
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
can be interpreted in conceptual terms as

\operatorname{HH}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)\cong\operatorname{H}^{p}\!\left(\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,q}\!\left(A,A\right)\right)=\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{p,q}\!\left(A,A\right),\quad p\geq 0,\ q\in\mathbb{Z},

where we regard
A
as an object of the derived category of graded

A
-bimodules
17
17

17

Which should not be confused with the derived category
of dg
A
-bimodules, with
A
viewed as a dg algebra with vanishing
differential.
. Here,

\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,q}\!\left(A,A\right)\coloneqq\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(A,A(q)\right),\qquad q\in\mathbb{Z},

is the derived
\operatorname{Hom}
functor in the derived category of graded

A
-bimodules and

\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{p,q}\!\left(A,A\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{p}\!\left(A,A(q)\right),\quad p\geq 0,\ q\in\mathbb{Z}.

The above identifications stem from isomorphisms of cochain complexes of
vector spaces

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,q}\!\left(A\right)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(\operatorname{B}(A)_{\bullet},A(q)\right),\qquad q\in\mathbb{Z},

where
\operatorname{B}(A)_{\bullet}
is the bar resolution of
A
, which is a graded projective
resolution of the latter as a graded
A
-bimodule. In terms of this
identification, the cup product on
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
corresponds to the (bigraded)
composition of endomorphisms in the derived category of graded
A
-bimodules
(equivalently, to the Yoneda product of extensions). On the other hand, to our
knowledge, there is no known interpretation of the Gerstenhaber bracket or of
the Gerstenhaber square in similar terms, although both admit an
interpretation in terms of ‘loop operations’ in the category of extensions,
see
[
Sch98
]
where the case of ungraded algebras is treated.

3.58
.

It is also useful to consider the Hochschild cochain complex when
A
is a
plain graded vector space, rather than a graded algebra. In this case the
pre-Lie product, the Gerstenhaber bracket and the Gerstenhaber square are
still defined, by the same formulas, as these only depend on the infinitesimal
composition of multilinear maps. In this case the name ‘Hochchild cochain
complex’ is a misnomer for it does not have a differential; notwithstanding,
we do not introduce any alternative terminology to distinguish this case.

4.
Bimodule Hochschild cohomology

In this section we recall the definition the bimodule Hochschild cohomology of a
graded bimodule that is introduced in
[
JM25
]
. We also study the effect of
the passage to shifts and linear duals on this cohomology, and also study the
case of the diagonal bimodule in more detail.

4.1.
The bimodule Hochschild cochain complex

Let
A
be a graded algebra and
M
a graded
A
-bimodule. We begin with an
auxiliary definition.

4.1
.

The
bimodule cochain complex

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is the bigraded vector space
with the bihomogeneous components

(4.2)

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{p+q=n}\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right),\qquad n\geq 0,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

where

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\coloneqq\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},M\right).

4.3
.

The horizontal degree
p+q
of a cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
does

not
agree with the ‘arity degree’
p+1+q
.

4.4
.

Endowed with a suitable bidegree
(1,0)
differential,
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
is a
differential bigraded vector space whose cohomology

(4.5)

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\right)=\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)

computes the bigraded vector space of graded-bimodule self-extensions of
M
.
This conceptual interpretation stems from isomorphisms of cochain complexes of
vector spaces

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\cong\mathbf{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(\operatorname{B}(A)_{\bullet}\otimes_{A}M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{B}(A)_{\bullet},M(r)\right),\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z},

where
\operatorname{B}(A)_{\bullet}
is the bar resolution of
A
. Moreover,
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is endowed
with an associative cup product that corresponds to the Yoneda product of
extensions, as well as with a natural graded
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule structure
induced, roughly speaking, by the monoidal structure on the derived category
of graded
A
-bimodules given by the derived tensor product over
A
. To keep
the exposition as brief as possible, we do not recall these structures
separately but rather deduce them from the algebraic structure of the bimodule
Hochschild cochain complex of
M
, see
4.43
.

4.6
.

The
bimodule Hochschild cochain complex
is the bigraded vector space

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
with the bihomogeneous components

(4.7)

\operatorname{C}^{n,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{C}^{n,r}\!\left(A\right)\oplus\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right),\qquad n\geq 0,\ r\in\mathbb{Z}.

4.8
.

Let
n\geq 0
. Viewed as a homogeneous element of
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
, the
horizontal degree of a cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
with
p+q=n-1
is
given by the expected ‘arity degree’
n=p+1+q
, compare with

Remark

4.3
.

4.9
.

We set

\operatorname{C}^{n}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\coloneqq\prod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{C}^{n,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right),\qquad n\geq 0.

Similar to
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
, the bimodule Hochschild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)

is a brace algebra with multiplication (see
Remark

4.29
). In
order to describe the bidegree
(-1,0)

bimodule pre-Lie product

(4.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\\
c_{1}\otimes c_{2}&\longrightarrow{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}\coloneqq{c_{1}}\left\{c_{2}\right\},\end{split}

where
c_{1},c_{2}
lie in either
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
or
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
, see

eq.

3.33
for the definition of
{c_{1}}\left\{c_{2}\right\}
. It is useful
to describe explicilty the bimodule pre-Lie product in the four natural cases:

•

If
c_{1},c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
, then
{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(A\right)
is
given by the pre-Lie product in the Hochschild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)

defined in
eq.

3.44
.

•

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
, then

{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}=0
.

•

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{s,t}\!\left(A\right)
, then

{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p+s-1,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\oplus\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q+s-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

is the cochain

(4.11)

{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}=\underbrace{\,\sum_{i=1}^{p}c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}\,}_{\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p+s-1,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)}+\underbrace{\,\sum_{j=1}^{q}c_{1}\bullet_{p+1+j}c_{2}\,}_{\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q+s-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)}.

•

Finally, if
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{s,t,u}\!\left(M,M\right)
,
then

{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}=c_{1}\bullet_{p+1}c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p+s,q+t,r+u}\!\left(M,M\right);

explicitly, using
eq.

3.28
we see that

(4.12)

({c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q+t})\coloneqq\\
(-1)^{\maltese}c_{1}(x_{1}\dots,x_{p},c_{2}(x_{p+1},\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{t}),y_{1+t},\dots,y_{q+t}),

with the sign given by

\textstyle\maltese\coloneqq(s+t)q+u\left(p+q+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|\right).

4.13
.

The pre-Lie product as defined by
eq.

4.10
is
ultimately determined by the infinitesimal composition operation defined by

eq.

3.28
. Consequently, there is an inconsistency
in the special case when
M=A
is the diagonal bimodule, since infinitesimal
compositions of the form
c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}
with
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
and

{c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A,A\right)}
do not automatically vanish, which is meant to be the case
in this context. This discrepancy can be fixed by replacing the diagonal
bimodule by a weighted copy, say
A\cdot\iota
where
\iota
is a formal
symbol, so that
A\neq A\cdot\iota
. Having clarified this important subtlety,
in the sequel we do not make this distinction explicit in order to not
overload the notation.

We now describe the algebraic structure on the Hochschild bimodule complex

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
as prescribed by
3.23
. Firstly,
the bidegree
(-1,0)

bimodule Gerstenhaber bracket

(4.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\\
c_{1}\otimes c_{2}&\longmapsto[c_{1},c_{2}]\coloneqq{c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}{c_{2}}\bullet{c_{1}},\end{split}

endows
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
with the structure of a shifted graded algebra with
respect to the total degree. If
\chark{\mathbf{k}}=2
or
n+r
is even, we also
consider the
bimodule Gerstenhaber square
operation

(4.15)

\Sq\colon\operatorname{C}^{n,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{2n-1,2r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right),\qquad c\longmapsto\Sq(c)\coloneqq{c}\bullet{c}.

4.16
.

It follows from the definition of the pre-Lie product on
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)

that
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\![1]
is a graded Lie ideal (defined in
the obvious way) and hence also a graded Lie subalgebra. Similarly,

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\![1]\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\![1]
is a graded Lie subalgebra.

4.17
.

Let
c\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
. For every
\iota\in\operatorname{C}^{0,*}\!\left(M\right)
, we have

{c}\bullet{\iota}=0={\iota}\bullet{c}

and hence

[c,\iota]={c}\bullet{\iota}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{\iota}\bullet{c}.

In particular,
[c,\mathrm{id}_{M}]=0
.

The bimodule Hochchild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is also endowed with
the brace algebra multiplication

(4.18)

m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}\coloneqq m_{2}^{A}+(m_{1,0}^{M}+m_{0,1}^{M})\in\operatorname{C}^{2,0}\!\left(A\right)\oplus(\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{1,0,0}\!\left(M,M\right)\oplus\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{0,1,0}\!\left(M,M\right)),

where
m_{2}^{A}
is the multiplication of
A
and

\displaystyle m_{1,0}^{M}\colon A\otimes M

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M,\qquad x\otimes m\longmapsto xm,

and

\displaystyle m_{0,1}^{M}\colon M\otimes A

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M,\qquad m\otimes y\longmapsto my,

are the action maps of the graded
A
-bimodule
M
. Equivalently,
m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}
is the square-zero product on the graded vector space
A\oplus M
:

(4.19)

(x,m)(x^{\prime},m^{\prime})\coloneqq(xx^{\prime},xm^{\prime}+mx^{\prime}),\qquad x,x^{\prime}\in A,\ m,m^{\prime}\in M.

Similar to the case of the multiplication
m_{2}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
, the fact that

m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a brace algebra multiplication is an
consequence of the associativity of the square-zero product. We obtain the
bidegree
(1,0)

bimodule Hochschild differential

(4.20)

d_{A|M}\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet+1,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right),\qquad c\longmapsto d_{A|M}(c)\coloneqq[m_{2}^{A\ltimes M},c],

and hence
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a differential bigraded vector space.

4.21
.

The
bimodule Hochschild cohomology of
M
is the cohomology of the
bimodule Hochchild cochain complex:

(4.22)

\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\right).

4.23
.

We set

\operatorname{HH}^{n}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\coloneqq\prod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{HH}^{n,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right),\qquad n\geq 0.

4.24
.

Endowed with the bimodule Hochschild differential,
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a
shifted dg Lie algebra and
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a shifted graded algebra, both
with respect to the total degree.

Finally, appealing once again to
3.23
, the
multiplication
m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}\in\operatorname{C}^{2,0}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
also induces the
bidegree
(0,0)

bimodule cup product

(4.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\\
c_{1}\otimes c_{2}&\longmapsto{c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}}\coloneqq(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}|}{m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\},\end{split}

where
c_{1},c_{2}
lie in either
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
or
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
. Keeping

eq.

3.38
in mind, let us describe the cochain

{m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}={m_{2}^{A}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}+{m_{1,0}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}+{m_{0,1}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}

in the four natural cases:

•

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{s,t}\!\left(A\right)
, then

\displaystyle{m_{2}^{A}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=(m_{2}^{A}\bullet_{1}c_{1})\bullet_{p+1}c_{2}=(-1)^{\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}}(m_{2}^{A}\bullet_{2}c_{2})\bullet_{1}c_{1}

\displaystyle{m_{1,0}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle{m_{0,1}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=0.

Consequently, the cup product
{c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}}
is the cup product in the
Hochschild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
defined in
eq.

3.54
.

•

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{s,t,u}\!\left(M,M\right)
, then

(4.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{m_{2}^{A}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0\\
{m_{1,0}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=(m_{1,0}^{M}\bullet_{1}c_{1})\bullet_{p+1}c_{2}\\
{m_{0,1}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0.\end{split}

Consequently, the cup product
{c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}}
lies in

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p+s,t,q+u}\!\left(M,M\right)
in this case and, using

eq.

3.40
, we see that it is given explicitly by

({c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{t})\coloneqq\\
(-1)^{u\left(p+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|\right)}\underbrace{c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})}_{\in A}\underbrace{c_{2}(x_{p+1},\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{t})}_{\in M}.

•

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{s,t}\!\left(A\right)
, then

(4.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{m_{2}^{A}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0\\
{m_{1,0}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0\\
{m_{0,1}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=(m_{0,1}^{M}\bullet_{1}c_{1})\bullet_{p+1+q+1}c_{2}\end{split}

Consequently, the cup product
{c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}}
lies in

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q+s,r+t}\!\left(M,M\right)
in this case and, using

eq.

3.40
, we see that it is given explicitly by

({c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{q+s})\coloneqq\\
(-1)^{\maltese}\underbrace{c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q})}_{\in M}\underbrace{c_{2}(y_{q+1},\dots,y_{q+s})}_{\in A},

with the sign given by

\textstyle\maltese\coloneqq t\left(p+1+q+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|+|m|+\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j}|\right).

•

Finally, if
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{s,t,u}\!\left(M,M\right)
,
then

(4.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{m_{2}^{A}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0\\
{m_{1,0}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0\\
{m_{0,1}^{M}}\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}&=0.\end{split}

Consequently,
{c_{1}}\cdot{c_{2}}=0
in this case.

With this structure,
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a dg algebra and its cohomology

\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a Gerstehaber algebra, in both cases with respect to the
total degree. Notice also that
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1]\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a
square-zero dg ideal.

4.29
.

The brace algebra structure on
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is obtained from the
infinitesimal composition operations of the shifted linear graded endomorphism
operad
[
BM09
]
of the pair
(A,M)
, which is isomorphic to the linear
endomorphism graded operad of the pair
(A,M(1))
.

4.30
.

The bimodule Hochschild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is functorial under
isomorphisms of graded
A
-bimodules. More precisely, given an isomorphism of
graded
A
-bimodules
\varphi\colon M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}N
the
maps

\displaystyle\Phi_{p,q}^{\varphi}\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(N,N\right),\qquad p,q\geq 0,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\longmapsto c^{\varphi},

where, for
x_{1},\dots,x_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{q}\in A
and
n\in N
,

c^{\varphi}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},n,y_{1},\dots,y_{q})\coloneqq\varphi(c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},\varphi^{-1}(n),y_{1},\dots,y_{q})),

assemble into an isomorphism of bigraded vector spaces

\Phi\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)}\oplus\Phi^{\varphi}\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,N\right)

that is strictly compatible with all of the algebraic structure described
above. Observe that the cochain
c^{\varphi}
is determined by the commutativity
of the following diagram of homogeneous morphisms of graded vector spaces:

(4.31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\otimes p}\otimes N\otimes A^{\otimes q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\varphi}}

4.32
.

It is also useful to consider the bimodule Hochschild cochain complex when
A

and
M
are a plain graded vector spaces. In this case the pre-Lie product,
the Gerstenhaber bracket and the Gerstenhaber square are still defined, by the
same formulas, as they only depend on the infinitesimal composition of
multilinear maps (compare with
Remark

3.58
).

We now describe in more detail the algebraic structure on the bimodule cochain
complex
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
and on the bimodule Hochschild cochain complex

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
. Firstly, notice that the bimodule Hochschild differential
decomposes as

(4.33)

d_{A|M}(c)=[m_{2}^{A},c]+[m_{1,0}^{A},c]+[m_{0,1}^{A},c],\qquad c\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right).

Thus, if
c\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
, then
[m_{2}^{A},c]=d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}(c)
and

(4.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[m_{1,0}^{A},c]&={m_{1,0}^{M}}\bullet{c}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\bullet{m_{1,0}^{M}}=m_{1,0}^{M}\bullet_{1}c,\\
[m_{0,1}^{A},c]&={m_{0,1}^{M}}\bullet{c}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\bullet{m_{0,1}^{M}}=m_{0,1}^{M}\bullet_{2}c.\end{split}

Moreover,

\displaystyle m_{1,0}^{M}\bullet_{1}c

\displaystyle=(m_{1,0}^{M}\bullet_{1}c)\bullet_{p+1}\mathrm{id}_{M}={m_{1,0}^{M}}\left\{c,\mathrm{id}_{M}\right\}=(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\cdot{\mathrm{id}_{M}},

\displaystyle m_{0,1}^{M}\bullet_{2}c

\displaystyle=(m_{0,1}^{M}\bullet_{1}\mathrm{id}_{M})\bullet_{2}c={m_{0,1}^{M}}\left\{\mathrm{id}_{M},c\right\}={\mathrm{id}_{M}}\cdot{c},

where we use that the shifted total degree of
\mathrm{id}_{M}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{0,0,0}\!\left(M,M\right)
is

|\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}|=|\mathrm{id}_{M}|-1=1-1=0.

Finally, if we identify

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)=(\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1])[1],

we can consider the map

(4.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\\
c&\longmapsto{\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}}\cdot{c}-{c}\cdot{\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}}=\mathbf{s}({\mathrm{id}_{M}}\cdot{c}-(-1)^{|c|}{c}\cdot{\mathrm{id}_{M}}),\end{split}

where we use the shifted graded
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule structure on
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
. On
the other hand, if
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
, then

(4.36)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[m_{2}^{A},c]&={m_{2}^{A}}\bullet{c}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\bullet{m_{2}^{A}}=(-1)^{|c|}{c}\bullet{m_{2}^{A}}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\\
[m_{1,0}^{M},c]&={m_{1,0}^{M}}\bullet{c}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\bullet{m_{1,0}^{M}}\\
&=m_{1,0}^{M}\bullet_{2}c-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}c\bullet_{p+1}m_{1,0}^{M}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\\
[m_{0,1}^{M},c]&={m_{0,1}^{M}}\bullet{c}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}{c}\bullet{m_{0,1}^{M}}\\
&=m_{0,1}^{M}\bullet_{1}c-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}c\bullet_{p+1}m_{0,1}^{M}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right).\end{split}

It follows that the bimodule Hochschild differential
d_{A|M}
has the form

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}&0\\
\delta&d_{\mathrm{Bim}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\oplus\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet+1,*}\!\left(A\right)\oplus\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right),

where the bidegree
(1,0)

bimodule differential

d_{\mathrm{Bim}}\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)

is the restriction of the bimodule Hochschild differential
d_{A|M}
to its
differential bigraded subspace
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
.
Explicitly, using
eq.

3.36
we see that

(4.38)

d_{\mathrm{Bim}}(c)=d_{\mathrm{h}}(c)+d_{\mathrm{v}}(c),\qquad c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

with the
horizontal bimodule differential

d_{\mathrm{h}}(c)\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p+1,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

given by

(4.39)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{h}}(c)(x_{1},\dots,x_{p+1},&m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q})\coloneqq(-1)^{r|x_{1}|+r}x_{1}c(x_{2},\dots,x_{p+1},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q})\\
&\phantom{=}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{i+r}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{i}x_{i+1},\dots,x_{p+1},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q})\\
&+(-1)^{p+1+r}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},x_{p+1}m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q}),\end{split}

and with the
vertical bimodule differential

d_{\mathrm{v}}(c)\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q+1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

given by

(4.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{v}}(c)(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},&m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q+1})\coloneqq(-1)^{p+1+r}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},my_{1},y_{2}\dots,y_{q+1})\\
&\phantom{=}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{p+1+j+r}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{j}y_{j+1},\dots,y_{q+1})\\
&+(-1)^{p+q+r}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q})y_{q+1}.\end{split}

4.41
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}
. The cochain complex
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,r}\!\left(M,N\right)
is the
totalisation of the apparent double cochain complex with anti-commuting
differentials:

d_{\mathrm{v}}d_{\mathrm{h}}+d_{\mathrm{h}}d_{\mathrm{v}}=0.

The following proposition summarises certain computational aspects of bimodule
Hochschild cohomology that can be derived from the previous discussion. Although
not all of these are needed in the sequel, we list some of them here to aid the
reader’s intuition and refer to
[
JM25
, Section 3]
for a more
thorough discussion.

4.42
[
JM25
, Remark 3.3.9, Lemma 3.3.12, Proposition 3.4.8, and
Corollary 3.4.9]
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}
. The following statements hold:

(1)

As a cochain complex,
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is the standard mapping
cocone of the cochain map

\delta\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,r}\!\left(A\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,r}\!\left(M,M\right),\qquad c\longmapsto{\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}}\cdot{c}-{c}\cdot{\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}}.

Moreover, the graded algebra structure on
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is precisely
that of the square-zero extension of
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
by the
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1]
.

(2)

There is an exact triangle in the derived category of dg vector spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

Moreover,

•

The map
p[1]\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\![1]\to\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\![1]
is a quotient map
of dg Lie algebras, and hence

i[1]\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1]\hookrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is the inclusion
of a dg Lie ideal and also of a dg Lie subalgebra.

•

The map
p\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\to\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a quotient map of dg
algebras, and hence the map map

i\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1]\hookrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is the inclusion of a
square-zero dg ideal and also of a dg
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule.

(3)

There is a long exact sequence of vector spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{n-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{n,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{n,*}\!\left(A\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{n+1,r}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{n+1,r}\!\left(A\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

Moreover:

•

The map
p\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\to\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is a morphism of
Gerstenhaber algebras (defined in the obvious way).

•

The map
i\colon\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\![-1]\to\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
is a morphism of
graded
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
-bimodules, where
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is viewed as a
graded
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
-bimodule via the quotient map
p
(equivalently,
through the shifted dg
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
-bimodule structure).

•

The connecting homomorphism
\delta
vanishes if and only if

\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is symmetric as a graded
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule:

{\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}}\cdot{c}={c}\cdot{\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}}\in\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right),\qquad c\in\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right),

where we use that
|\mathbf{s}\mathrm{id}_{M}|=|\mathrm{id}_{M}|-1=0
since

\mathrm{id}_{M}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{0,0,0}\!\left(M,M\right)
.

We also record the following result that is of independent interest.

Definition-Proposition 4.43

(
[
JM25
, Lemma 3.2.12]
)
.

The following statements hold:

(1)

Endowed with the shifted bimodule differential
d_{\mathrm{Bim}}[1]
and the

Yoneda product
18
18

18

Here, we identify
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
with a
differential bigraded subspace of
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\![1]
. Notice also that

|\mathbf{s}c_{1}\circ\mathbf{s}c_{2}|=|\mathbf{s}({c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}})|=|c_{1}|+|c_{2}|-2=|\mathbf{s}c_{1}|+|\mathbf{s}c_{2}|,

so that the Yoneda product is indeed graded.

(4.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\\
\mathbf{s}c_{1}\otimes\mathbf{s}c_{2}&\longmapsto{\mathbf{s}c_{1}}\circ{\mathbf{s}c_{2}}\coloneqq\mathbf{s}({c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}),\end{split}

see
eq.

4.12
, the bimodule cochain complex

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is a dg algebra with respect to the total degree.

(2)

The graded commutator

(4.45)

[\mathbf{s}c_{1},\mathbf{s}c_{2}]={\mathbf{s}c_{1}}\circ{\mathbf{s}c_{2}}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}{\mathbf{s}c_{1}}\circ{\mathbf{s}c_{1}}

of the Yoneda product agrees with the restriction of the bimodule
Gerstenhaber bracket to the dg Lie ideal

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\![1]
.

(3)

Endowed with the graded
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule structure given by the shifted
bimodule cup product

\displaystyle\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle c_{1}\otimes\mathbf{s}c_{2}\otimes c_{3}

\displaystyle\longmapsto(-1)^{|c_{1}|}\mathbf{s}({c_{1}}\cdot{{c_{2}}\cdot{c_{3}}}),

the bimodule cochain complex
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is a dg
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule with
respect to the total degree. Consequently, the bigraded vector space of
bimodule self-extensions
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is a graded
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule with
respect to the total degree.

4.46
.

The action of
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
on the bigraded bimodule self-extension space

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
described in
4.43
can be understood in conceptual
terms as follows. Recall that the derived category of graded
A
-bimodules is
a monoidal category with respect to the derived tensor product
(M,N)\mapsto M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}N
. The tensor product of morphisms induces maps

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(A,A\right)\otimes\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(A\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}M,A\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}M\right)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M,M\right)\otimes\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(A,A\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A,M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A\right).

Applying the structural isomorphisms
A\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}{M}\cong M
and

M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}{A}\cong M
(and similarly for
M
), we obtain maps maps

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(A,A\right)\otimes\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M,M\right)\otimes\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(A,A\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\!\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{e}}\!\left(M,M\right)

that induce the required action maps

\displaystyle\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\otimes\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)\otimes\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right),

Since the (derived) tensor product over
A
is not symmetric in general, the
left and right actions of
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
on
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
might be quite different;
more precisely
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
need not be a symmetric
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule, see

Proposition

4.42
. It is also worth mentioning that
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(=strong homotoypy Lie algebra) structures on mapping cones have been
considered previously in the literature, see
[
FM07
]
and the references
therein.

4.47
.

Let
M
and
N
be graded
A
-bimodules. The bimodule cochain complex admits
an apparent generalisation

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,N\right)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{p+q=n}\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},N\right),\qquad n\geq 1,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

whose cohomology is the bigradeded space of graded-bimodule extensions,

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,N\right)\right)=\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,N\right).

The formula for the bimodule differential is entirely analogous, and so are
the formulas for the left and right actions of the Hochchild cochain complex

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
. In particular,
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,N\right)
is a graded
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule. Notice,
however, that
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,N\right)
does not admit a Yoneda product when
M\not\cong N
, although there is Yoneda product of the form

\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,N\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(L,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(L,N\right),

where
L
is a further graded
A
-bimodule, which corresponds to the bigraded
composition of morphisms in the derived category of graded
A
-bimodules. As
with the bimodule Hochschild cochain complex, we also consider the bimodule
complex when
A
,
M
and
N
are plain graded vector spaces, in which case
there is no natural differential.

4.2.
Passage to vertical shifts of a graded module

Let
A
be a graded algebra and
M
a graded
A
-bimodule. Fix
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. We
wish to show that the bimodule Hochschild cochain complex is invariant under the
passage
M\mapsto M(n)
from
M
to its (vertical) shift. For
p,q\geq 0
and

r\in\mathbb{Z}
, we introduce the isomorphism of vector spaces

\displaystyle\Psi_{p,q,r}^{(n)}\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M(n),M(n)\right)

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\longmapsto c^{(n)},

where, for
x_{1}\dots,x_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{q}\in A
and
m\in M
,

(4.48)

c^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},\mathbf{s}^{n}m,y_{1},\dots y_{1})\coloneqq\\
(-1)^{n(\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|)}\mathbf{s}^{n}c(x_{1}\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q}).

In other words, the cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M(n),M(n)\right)
is defined by the
commutativity of the following diagram, in which the top horizontal map is an
instance of the isomorphism of dg vector spaces in
Example

2.14
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\otimes p}\otimes M(n)\otimes A^{\otimes q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q})(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(n)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{(n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c(n)}

We then define the map

(4.49)

\Psi\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)}\oplus\Psi^{(n)}\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M(n)\right),

where

\Psi^{(n)}\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{m-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{m-1,r}\!\left(M(n),M(n)\right),\qquad m\geq 0,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

acts on a cochain
c=(c_{p,q})_{p+1+q=m}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{m-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
by

\Psi^{(n)}(c)\coloneqq(c_{p,q}^{(n)})_{p+1+q=m}.

4.50
.

For a cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{0,0,*}\!\left(M,M\right)=\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}\!\left(M,M\right)
, we have

c^{(n)}=c[n]
, see
2.13
.

4.51
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. The isomorphism of bigraded vector spaces

\displaystyle\Psi\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M(n)\right),

is strictly compatible with the following algebraic structures on both source
and target:
19
19

19

In fact,
\Psi
is an isomorphism of brace algebras with
multiplication.
the bimodule pre-Lie product, the bimodule Gerstenhaber
bracket, the bimodule Gerstenhaber square, the brace algebra multiplication,
the bimodule Hochschild differential, and the bimodule cup product.
Consequently, the induced map

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\Psi\right)\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M(n)\right)

is an isomorphism of Gerstenhaber algebras with respect to the total degree.

Proof.

Firstly, let
c_{1},c_{2}
lie in either
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
or
\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
. A
straightforward case-by-case computation shows that

\Psi(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})=\Psi(c_{1})\bullet_{i}\Psi(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}.

Since the algebraic structure on the bimodule Hochschild cochain complex is
defined completely in terms of the infinitesimal compositions, it only remains
to show that
\Psi
preserves the brace algebra multiplication:

\Psi(m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})=m_{2}^{A\ltimes M(n)}.

Indeed,

\Psi(m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:square-zero_product-AM}}}{{=}}\Psi(m_{2}^{A}+m_{1,0}^{M}+m_{0,1}^{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Phi}}}{{=}}m_{2}^{A}+\Psi(m_{1,0}^{M})+\Psi(m_{0,1}^{M}).

Moreover, since
\lvert m_{1,0}^{M}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}=0
, for
x\in A
and
m\in M
,

\displaystyle\Psi(m_{1,0}^{M})(x,\mathbf{s}^{n}m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Phi}}}{{=}}(-1)^{n|x|}\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1,0}^{M}(x,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n|x|}\mathbf{s}^{n}xm

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Mn}}}{{=}}m_{1,0}^{M(n)}(x,\mathbf{s}^{n}m).

Similarly,
\Psi(m_{1,0}^{M})=m_{0,1}^{M(n)}
. Therefore,

\Psi(m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})=m_{2}^{A}+m_{1,0}^{M(n)}+m_{0,1}^{M(n)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:square-zero_product-AM}}}{{=}}m_{2}^{A\ltimes M(n)}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

4.52
.

Proposition

4.51
remains valid when
A
and
M
are plain graded
vector spaces, keeping in mind that the brace algebra multiplication,
Hochchild differential and cup product are not defined in this case.

4.53
.

Let
A
,
M
and
N
be graded vector spaces (for example a graded algebra and
two graded bimodules over it). For
p,q\geq 0
we introduce the isomorphism of
vector spaces

\Psi_{p,q,r}^{(n)}\colon\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},N\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M(n)\otimes A^{\otimes q},N(n)\right),

defined using
eq.

4.48
. We then have

\Psi(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})=\Psi(c_{1})\bullet_{i}\Psi(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}},

whenever both infinitesimal compositions vanish, but also in the following
cases:

•

c_{1}\in\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},N\right)

and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
.

•

c_{1}\in\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},N\right)

and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
.

•

c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(N,N\right)
and
c_{2}\in\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},N\right)
.

4.3.
Passage to the linear dual of a graded bimodule

Let
A
be a graded algebra and
M
a graded
A
-bimodule. We now analyse how
does the bimodule cochain complex interact with the passage from
M
to its
linear dual
DM
. For
{p,q\geq 0}
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}
, we introduce the morphism of
vector spaces (notice the transposition
(p,q,r)\mapsto(q,p,r)
of the first two
components)

\displaystyle\Theta_{p,q,r}^{D}\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{q,p,r}\!\left(DM,DM\right),\qquad p,q\geq 0,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\longmapsto c^{D},

where, for
x_{1}\dots,x_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{q}\in A
,
f\in DM
and
m\in M
,

(4.54)

c^{D}(y_{1},\dots y_{q},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(m)\coloneqq(-1)^{\maltese}f(c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots y_{q})),

and

\textstyle\maltese\coloneqq(p+1)(q+1)+\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}|f|+(\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j}|)(|f|+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|+|m|).

In other words, the cochain
c^{D}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{q,p,r}\!\left(DM,DM\right)
is defined by the
commutativity, up to the sign
(-1)^{(p+1)(q+1)}
, of the following diagram of
homogeneous morphisms of graded vector spaces, in which the top horizontal
isomorphism uses the braiding corresponding to the permutation

(\begin{smallmatrix}1&2&3&4\\
4&1&2&3)\end{smallmatrix}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\otimes q}\otimes DM\otimes A^{\otimes p}\otimes M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces DM\otimes A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces DM\otimes M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces DM\otimes M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{D}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\otimes c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}}

We then define the map

(4.55)

\Theta\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)}\oplus\Theta^{D}\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,DM\right),

where

\Theta^{D}\colon\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n-1,r}\!\left(DM,DM\right),\qquad n\geq 0,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

acts on a cochain
c=(c_{p,q})_{p+1+q=n}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{n-1,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
by

\Theta^{D}(c)\coloneqq(c_{p,q}^{D})_{q+1+p=n}.

4.56
.

For a cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{0,0,*}\!\left(M,M\right)=\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}\!\left(M,M\right)
, we have
c^{D}=-c^{*}

(notice the minus sign), see
Example

2.11
. For example, if
d\colon M\to M
is a degree
1
differential, for
f\in DM
,

d^{D}(f)(m)=-(-1)^{|f|}f(d(m)),\qquad m\in M,

compare with
Example

2.10
.

4.57
.

The morphism of bigraded vector spaces

\displaystyle\Theta\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,DM\right),

satisfies the following compatibilities with respect to the infinitesimal
compositions:

(1)

If
c_{1},c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
, then

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})=c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{i}\Theta(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}.

(2)

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
and

c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet-1,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
, then

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})=0=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{i}\Theta(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}.

(3)

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
,
then

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1+j}c_{2})=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{j}\Theta(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq j\leq q,

and

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{q+1+i}\Theta(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq p.

(4)

If
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and

c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{s,t,u}\!\left(M,M\right)
, then

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1}c_{2})=-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}\Theta(c_{2})\bullet_{t+1}\Theta(c_{1}),

where
|\mathbf{s}c_{1}|=p+q+r-1
is the shifted total degree, and similarly for
|\mathbf{s}c_{2}|
.

Proof.

The first two statements are obvious since
\Theta
acts as the identity on
the subspace
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
, see
eq.

4.55
.

We continue by establishing statement (
3
). Let

c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}^{s,t}\!\left(A\right)
and
1\leq j\leq q
. We
compute, for
x_{1},\dots,x_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{q+s}\in A
,
f\in DM
and
m\in M
,

\displaystyle(\Theta

\displaystyle(c_{1})\bullet_{j}c_{2})(y_{1},\dots,y_{q+s},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{1}}c_{1}^{D}(y_{1},\dots,y_{j-i},c_{2}(y_{j},\dots,y_{j+s-1}),y_{j+s},\dots,y_{q+s},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{1}+\maltese_{2}}f(c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{j-i},c_{2}(y_{j},\dots,y_{j+s-1}),y_{j+s},\dots,y_{q+s})).

where the signs are given by

\displaystyle\maltese_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}\textstyle(s-1)(p+1+q-j)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(p+q+\sum_{i=1}^{j-i}|y_{i}|\right)

\displaystyle=\textstyle(s-1)(p+1)+(s-1)(q-j)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(p+q+\sum_{i=1}^{j-i}|y_{i}|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}\textstyle(p+1)(q+1)+|f|\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\left(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{k=1}^{q+s}|y_{k}|\right)\left(|f|+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p}|x_{\ell}|+|m|\right).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\Theta

\displaystyle(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1+j}c_{2})(y_{1},\dots,y_{q+s},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{3}}f((c_{1}\bullet_{p+1+j}c_{2})(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{q+s}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{3}+\maltese_{4}}f(c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m,y_{1},\dots,y_{j-i},c_{2}(y_{j},\dots,y_{j+s-1}),y_{j+s},\dots,y_{q+s})),

where the signs are given by

\displaystyle\maltese_{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}\textstyle(p+1)(q+s)+|f|(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}})+\left(\sum_{k=1}^{q+s}|y_{k}|\right)\left(|f|+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p}|x_{\ell}|+|m|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}\textstyle(s-1)(q-j)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(p+q+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p}|x_{p}|+|m|+\sum_{i=1}^{j-i}|y_{i}|\right).

Comparing the signs we see that

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1+j}c_{2})=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{j}c_{2}=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{j}\Theta(c_{2}).

An entirely analogous computation shows that

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2})=\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{q+1+i}\Theta(c_{2}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq p.

We finish the proof be establishing statement (
4
).
Let
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{s,t,u}\!\left(M,M\right)
. For

x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s},y_{1}\dots,y_{q+t}\in A
,
f\in DM
and
m\in M
we compute

\displaystyle\Theta

\displaystyle(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1}c_{2})(y_{1}\dots,y_{q+t},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s})(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{5}}f((c_{1}\bullet_{p+1}c_{2})(x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{q+t}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{5}+\maltese_{6}}f(c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},c_{2}(x_{p+1}\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{t}),y_{1+t},\dots,y_{q+t})),

where the signs are given by

\displaystyle\maltese_{5}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(q+t+1)(p+s+1)+\textstyle|f|(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}})+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{q+t}|y_{j}|\right)\left(|f|+\sum_{i=1}^{p+s}|x_{i}|+|m|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{6}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}\textstyle(s+t)q+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(p+q+\sum_{k=1}^{p}|x_{k}|\right).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle(\Theta

\displaystyle(c_{2})\bullet_{t+1}\Theta(c_{1}))(y_{1}\dots,y_{q+t},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p+s})(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{7}}c_{2}^{D}(y_{1}\dots,y_{t},c_{1}^{D}(y_{1+t},\dots,y_{q+t},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),x_{p+1}\dots,x_{p+s})(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{7}+\maltese_{8}}c_{1}^{D}(y_{1+t},\dots,y_{q+t},f,x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(c_{2}(x_{p+1}\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{t}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\maltese_{7}+\maltese_{8}+\maltese_{9}}f(c_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},c_{2}(x_{p+1}\dots,x_{p+s},m,y_{1}\dots,y_{t}),y_{1+t},\dots,y_{q+t})),

where the signs are given by

\displaystyle\maltese_{7}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:infinitesimal_composition}}}{{=}}\textstyle(p+q)s+\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(s+t+\sum_{i=1}^{t}|y_{i}|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{8}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}\textstyle(s+1)(t+1)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\left(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j+t}|+|f|+\sum_{k=1}^{p}|x_{k}|\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\phantom{=}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t}|y_{i}|\right)\left(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j+t}|+|f|+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p+s}|x_{\ell}|+|m|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{9}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}\textstyle(p+1)(q+1)+\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}|f|

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\textstyle+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j+t}|\right)\left(|f|+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p+s}|x_{\ell}|+|m|+\sum_{i=1}^{t}|y_{i}|\right).

Comparing the signs we see that

\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1}c_{2})=-(-1)^{\maltese}\Theta(c_{2})\bullet_{s+1}\Theta(c_{1}),

with,

\displaystyle\maltese

\displaystyle=\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}(s+t)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}(p+q)+(p+q)(s+t)

\displaystyle=\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-1)+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-1)+(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-1)(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}-1)

\displaystyle=(\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}+\lvert c_{1}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}-1)(\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{h}}+\lvert c_{2}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}-1)

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|.

This finishes the proof.
∎

4.58
.

The morphism of bigraded vector spaces

\displaystyle\Theta\colon\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,DM\right),

is a morphism of differential bigraded vector spaces. Moreover,
\Theta
is
strictly compatible with the brace algebra multiplication, the bimodule
Gerstenhaber bracket and the bimodule Gerstehaber square. In particular,

\Theta
induces a morphism of shifted graded Lie algebras

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\Theta\right)\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,DM\right)

with respect to the total degree that also preserves the induced Gerstenhaber
square operation.

Proof.

We verify first that
\Theta
preserves the brace algebra multiplication.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\Theta(m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:square-zero_product-AM}}}{{=}}\Theta(m_{2}^{A}+m_{1,0}^{M}+m_{0,1}^{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Theta}}}{{=}}m_{2}^{A}+\Theta(m_{1,0}^{M})+\Theta(m_{0,1}^{M}).

Moreover, since
m_{1,0}^{M}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{1,0,0}\!\left(M,M\right)
, for
x\in A
,
f\in DM

and
m\in M
,

\displaystyle\Theta(m_{1,0}^{M})(f,x)(m)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}f(m_{1,0}^{M}(x,m))=f(xm)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Mn}}}{{=}}m_{0,1}^{DM}(f,x)(m).

Similarly, keeping in mind that
\lvert m_{0,1}^{M}\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}=0
, for
y\in A
,
f\in DM
and
m\in M
we have

\displaystyle\Theta(m_{0,1}^{M})(y,f)(m)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cD}}}{{=}}(-1)^{|y|(|f|+|m|)}f(m_{0,1}^{M}(m,y))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|y|(|f|+|m|)}f(my)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Mn}}}{{=}}m_{0,1}^{DM}(y,f)(m).

Therefore

\Theta(m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})=m_{2}^{A}+m_{0,1}^{DM}+m_{1,0}^{DM}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:square-zero_product-AM}}}{{=}}m_{2}^{A\ltimes DM},

as required.

We continue the proof by showing that
\Theta
is strictly compatible with the
bimodule Gerstehaber bracket and the bimodule Gerstenhaber square. Since

\Theta
acts as the identity on
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
, see

eq.

4.55
, it is obvious that

\displaystyle\Theta([c_{1},c_{2}])

\displaystyle=[\Theta(c_{1}),\Theta(c_{2})]

\displaystyle c_{1},c_{2}

\displaystyle\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right),

\displaystyle\Theta(\Sq(c))

\displaystyle=\Sq(\Theta(c))

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right).

We now treat the two remaining cases (recall that the Lie bracket is graded
anti-symmetric with respect to the shifted total degree):

•

For
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
,

\displaystyle\Theta([c_{1},c_{2}])

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstehaber_bracket-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Theta({c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}\underbrace{{c_{2}}\bullet{c_{1}}}_{=0})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pre-Lie_product-RelBimHC-split}}}{{=}}\textstyle\Theta(\sum_{i=1}^{p}c_{1}\bullet_{i}c_{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}c_{1}\bullet_{p+1+q}c_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {lemma:Theta}\eqref{eq:Theta-BimHC-HC}}}{{=}}\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{q+1+i}\Theta(c_{2})+\sum_{j=1}^{q}\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{j}\Theta(c_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pre-Lie_product-RelBimHC-split}}}{{=}}{\Theta(c_{1})}\bullet{\Theta(c_{2})}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}\underbrace{{\Theta(c_{2})}\bullet{\Theta(c_{1})}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstehaber_bracket-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}[\Theta(c_{1}),\Theta(c_{2})].

•

For
c_{1}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
and
c_{2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{s,t,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
,

\displaystyle\Theta([c_{1},c_{2}])

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstehaber_bracket-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Theta({c_{1}}\bullet{c_{2}}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}{c_{2}}\bullet{c_{1}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pre-Lie_product-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Theta(c_{1}\bullet_{p+1}c_{2}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}c_{2}\bullet_{s+1}c_{1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {lemma:Theta}\eqref{eq:Theta-BimHC-BimHC}}}{{=}}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}\Theta(c_{2})\bullet_{t+1}\Theta(c_{1})+\Theta(c_{1})\bullet_{q+1}\Theta(c_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pre-Lie_product-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}{\Theta(c_{1})}\bullet{\Theta(c_{2})}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c_{1}||\mathbf{s}c_{2}|}{\Theta(c_{2})}\bullet{\Theta(c_{1})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstehaber_bracket-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}[\Theta(c_{1}),\Theta(c_{2})].

•

For
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
, when
\chark{\mathbf{k}}=2
or
|c|=|\mathbf{s}c|+1
is
even,

\displaystyle\Theta(\Sq(c))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_square-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Theta({c}\bullet{c})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pre-Lie_product-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Theta(c\bullet_{p+1}c)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {lemma:Theta}\eqref{eq:Theta-BimHC-BimHC}}}{{=}}-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}c|}\Theta(c)\bullet_{q+1}\Theta(c)

\displaystyle=\Theta(c)\bullet_{q+1}\Theta(c)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pre-Lie_product-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}{\Theta(c)}\bullet{\Theta(c)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_square-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Sq(\Theta(c)).

Finally, we prove that
\Theta
commutes with the bimodule Hochschild
differential. Indeed, for
c\in\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
,

\displaystyle\Theta(d_{A|M}(c))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Hd-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}\Theta([m_{2}^{A\ltimes M},c])

\displaystyle=[\Theta(m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}),\Theta(c)]

\displaystyle=[m_{2}^{A\ltimes DM},\Theta(c)]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Hd-RelBimHC}}}{{=}}d_{A|DM}(\Theta(c)).

This finishes the proof.
∎

4.59
.

Lemma

4.57
and
Proposition

4.58
remain valid when
A

and
M
are plain graded vector spaces, keeping in mind that the brace algebra
multiplication, Hochchild differential and cup product are not defined in this
case.

4.60
.

Let
A
,
M
and
N
be graded vector spaces (for example a graded algebra and
two graded bimodules over it). For
p,q\geq 0
we introduce the morphism

\Theta_{p,q,r}^{D}\colon\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes M\otimes A^{\otimes q},N\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{k}}^{r}\!\left(A^{\otimes p}\otimes DN\otimes A^{\otimes q},DM\right),

defined using
eq.

4.54
. Then, the obvious variants of the compatibilities
in
Lemma

4.57
hold, compare with
4.53
.

4.4.
Bimodules isomorphic to their vertically-shifted linear dual

Let
A
be a graded algebra and
M
a graded
A
-bimodule. We are interested in
the case when
M(n)\cong DM
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. We begin with a basic
observation concerning such graded bimodules. We refer the reader
to
[
Kel08
, Sec. 2.6]
, Van den Bergh’s
[
Boc08
, Appendix A]

and
[
RRZ17
]
for closely related discussions concerning Serre functors on
triangulated categories in the sense of
[
BK89
]
.

4.61
.

Suppose that there exists an isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules

\varphi\colon M(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DM

for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then, the induced non-degenerate bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle=\langle-,-\rangle_{\varphi}\colon M(n)\times M\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1},m_{2})\longmapsto\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1})(m_{2})

satisfies the following equation for all
x,y\in A
and
m_{1},m_{2}\in M
:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}xm_{1}y,m_{2}\rangle=(-1)^{|x|(|m_{1}|+|y|+|m_{2}|)}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1},ym_{2}x\rangle.

Proof.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}xm_{1}y,m_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}xm_{1}y)(m_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n|x|}\varphi(x\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1}y)(m_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n|x|+|x|(|m_{1}|-n+|y|+|m_{2}|)}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1})(ym_{2}x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|x|(|m_{1}|+|y|+|m_{2}|)}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1},ym_{2}x\rangle.\qed

4.62
.

In
Proposition

4.61
, notice that whenever
|m_{1}|+|m_{2}|\neq n
we must
have

\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1},m_{2}\rangle=\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1})(m_{2})=0.

Indeed,
\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1}\in M(n)^{|m_{1}|-n}
and hence

\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1})\in(DM)^{|m_{1}|-n}=D(M^{n-|m_{1}|}).

4.63
.

Suppose that there exists an isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules

\varphi\colon A(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DA

for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then, the induced non-degenerate bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle=\langle-,-\rangle_{\varphi}\colon A(n)\times A\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(\mathbf{s}^{n}x,y)\longmapsto\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}x)(y)

is associative,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}xy,z\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x,yz\rangle

\displaystyle x,y,z

\displaystyle\in A,

and graded-symmetric with respect to the degree in
A
,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x,y\rangle

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|x||y|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}y,x\rangle

\displaystyle x,y

\displaystyle\in A.

Proof.

Both claims follows readily from
Proposition

4.61
. Associativity is
clear and to exhibit the graded-symmetry we compute

\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x,y\rangle=\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x\cdot 1,y\rangle=(-1)^{|x|(|1|+|y|)}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}1,yx\rangle=(-1)^{|x||y|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}y,x\rangle.\qed

4.64
.

Suppose that there exists an isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules

\varphi\colon M(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DM

for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. The reader can easily verify, using for example

Example

2.33
or by direct computation, that the map

\psi\colon M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(M(n)),\qquad m\longmapsto(-1)^{n|m|}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m)\mathbf{s}^{-n},

is also an isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules. By adjunction, we obtain a
non-degenerate bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle_{\psi}\colon M\times M(n)\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(m_{1},m_{2})\longmapsto\psi(m_{1})(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{2}),

that by construction satisfies

\langle m_{1},\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{2}\rangle_{\psi}=(-1)^{n|m_{1}|}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1})(m_{2})=(-1)^{n|m_{1}|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}m_{1},m_{2}\rangle_{\varphi},

so that the values of both pairings agree whenever
n
is even (note that the
construction of these two pairings only needs that
M
is a graded vector
space). When
M=A
is the diagonal
A
-bimodule, if
n
is odd the new pairing
is only associative up to a sign:

\langle xy,\mathbf{s}^{n}z\rangle_{\psi}=(-1)^{|x|+|y|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}xy,z\rangle_{\varphi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {cor:graded-symmetric-pairing}}}{{=}}(-1)^{|x|+|y|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x,yz\rangle_{\varphi}=(-1)^{|y|}\langle x,\mathbf{s}^{n}yz\rangle_{\psi}.

Still for
n
odd, the new pairing is anti-symmetric with respect to the total
degree,

\langle x,\mathbf{s}^{n}y\rangle_{\psi}+(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}x||\mathbf{s}y|}\langle y,\mathbf{s}^{n}x\rangle_{\psi}=0,

compare with
[
Cho08
, Section 2]
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\langle x,\mathbf{s}^{n}y\rangle_{\psi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|x|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x,y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {cor:graded-symmetric-pairing}}}{{=}}(-1)^{|x|+|x||y|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}y,x\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|x|+|x||y|+|y|}\langle y,\mathbf{s}^{n}x\rangle_{\psi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(|x|+1)(|y|+1)-1}\langle y,\mathbf{s}^{n}x\rangle_{\psi}

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{|\mathbf{s}x||\mathbf{s}y|}\langle y,\mathbf{s}^{n}x\rangle_{\psi}.

Definition-Proposition 4.65
.

Suppose that there exists an isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules

\varphi\colon M(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DM

for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and consider the isomorphism

\psi\colon M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(M(n)),\qquad m\longmapsto m^{*}\coloneqq(-1)^{n|m|}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}m)\mathbf{s}^{-n},

defined in
Remark

4.64
. We introduce the morphism of differential
bigraded vector spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M(n)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,D(M(n))\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}

obtained as the composite of the morphisms defined by

eqs.

4.31
,
4.55
and
4.49
(the latter for the inverse isomorphism

\psi^{-1}
). Explicitly,
\Upsilon
acts as the identity on the subspace

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\subseteq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
and, to a cochain
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

it associates the cochain (notice the transposition
(p,q,r)\mapsto(q,p,r)
of
the first two components)

\Upsilon(c)\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{q,p,r}\!\left(M,M\right)

that is uniquely determined by the requirement that the following equality
holds for all homogeneous elements
x_{1},\dots,x_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{q}\in A
and

m,m^{\prime}\in M
:

(4.66)

\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\Upsilon(c)(y_{1},\dots,y_{q},m,x_{1},\dots,x_{q}),m^{\prime}\rangle_{\varphi}\\
=(-1)^{(p+1)(q+1)+\maltese}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m^{\prime},y_{1},\dots,y_{q}),m\rangle_{\varphi},

where

\maltese\coloneqq\textstyle(|m|+\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{q}|)(|m^{\prime}|+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|).

The morphism
\Psi
is strictly compatible with the Gerstenhaber bracket and
the Gerstenhaber square operation.

Proof.

Indeed, for
c\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{p,q,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
the cochain

\Upsilon(c)\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{q,p,r}\!\left(M,M\right)
is determined by the requirement that

\displaystyle\langle\Upsilon(c)(y_{1}\dots,y_{q},m,x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),\mathbf{s}^{n}m^{\prime}\rangle_{\psi}=(c^{(n)})^{D}(y_{1}\dots,y_{q},m^{*},x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(\mathbf{s}^{n}m^{\prime}).

The left-hand side can be written instead as

\displaystyle\langle\Upsilon(c)(y_{1}\dots,y_{q},m,x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),\mathbf{s}^{n}m^{\prime}\rangle_{\psi}=(-1)^{\maltese_{0}}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\Upsilon(c)(y_{1}\dots,y_{q},m,x_{1},\dots,x_{p}),m^{\prime}\rangle_{\varphi},

where

\maltese_{0}=n(\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|+|m|+\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j}|).

The right-hand side is given by

\displaystyle(c^{(n)})^{D}

\displaystyle(y_{1}\dots,y_{q},m^{*},x_{1},\dots,x_{p})(\mathbf{s}^{n}m^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\maltese_{1}}m^{*}(c^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},\mathbf{s}^{n}m^{\prime},y_{1},\dots,y_{p}))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\maltese_{1}+\maltese_{2}}m^{*}(\mathbf{s}^{n}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m^{\prime},y_{1},\dots,y_{p}))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\maltese_{1}+\maltese_{2}}\langle m,\mathbf{s}^{n}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m^{\prime},y_{1},\dots,y_{p})\rangle_{\psi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\maltese_{1}+\maltese_{2}+n|m|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}m,c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m^{\prime},y_{1},\dots,y_{p})\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\maltese_{1}+\maltese_{2}+\maltese_{3}+n|m|}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}c(x_{1},\dots,x_{p},m^{\prime},y_{1},\dots,y_{p}),m\rangle_{\varphi},

where

\displaystyle\maltese_{1}

\displaystyle=\textstyle(p+1)(q+1)+\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}|m|+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j}|\right)\left(|m|+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|-n+|m^{\prime}|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle n\left(\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|\right)

\displaystyle\maltese_{3}

\displaystyle=\textstyle|m|\left(\lvert c\rvert_{\mathrm{v}}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|x_{i}|+|m^{\prime}|+\sum_{j=1}^{q}|y_{j}|\right).

The claim follows since, modulo
2
,

\maltese=\maltese_{0}+\maltese_{1}+\maltese_{2}+\maltese_{3}+n|m|.

The strict compatibility between
\Upsilon
and the Gerstenhaber bracket and
the Gerstenhaber square operation follows immediately from

Remark

4.30
and

Propositions

4.51
and
4.58
. This finishes the
proof.
∎

4.67
.

4.65
remains valid when
A
and
M
are graded vector spaces
and
\varphi
is only an isomorphism of such, see also

Remarks

4.52
and
4.59
.

4.5.
The bimodule Hochschild cohomology of the diagonal bimodule

Let
A
be a graded algebra. In this article we are chiefly interested in the
bimodule Hochschild cochain complex
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,A\right)
, which turns out to admit
a very natural description.

4.68
.

We introduce the bigraded vector space

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\![\varepsilon]\coloneqq\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\oplus(\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\cdot\varepsilon)

where
\varepsilon
is bidegree
(1,0)
element, so that we may identify

\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\cdot\varepsilon=\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\![-1]
