\varphi\oplus\psi\colon(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\oplus\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\ltimes\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(A\oplus M,m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})

is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-algebras, where

m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}
is the square-zero product on
A\oplus M
.

5.68
.

Let
A
be a dg algebra and
M
a dg
A
-bimodule. Choose strong deformation
retractions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{A}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{M}}

By forming their direct sum, these determine a strong deformation retraction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\oplus\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{A\ltimes M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{A\ltimes M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{A\ltimes M}}

where we now view
A\oplus M
as a dg algebra with the square-zero product

m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}
.
5.63
then yields a minimal

A_{\infty}
-algebra structure
m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\ltimes\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}
on the graded vector

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\oplus\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)
which, by construction, satisfies the conditions in

Proposition

5.37
25
25

25

Indeed, the square-zero product

m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}
exhibits
M\subseteq A\oplus M
as a square-zero
ideal.
and hence it encodes a minimal

on

over the minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra

(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)})
. Moreover, it follows immediately from the explicit
formulas for extending

i^{A\ltimes M}=i^{A}\oplus i^{M}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p^{A\ltimes M}=p^{A}\oplus p^{M}

to mutually homotopy-inverse
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms established
in

]
that in this way one obtains a minimal model of the pair

(A,M)
in the sense of

5.67
.

5.69
.

or one of the action maps
m_{1,0}^{M}
or
m_{0,1}^{M}

Also, the root vertex now needs to be labeled by

. For
example, the marked decorated PBT

p^{M}
i^{A}
i^{A}
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i^{M}

describes the operation

p^{M}\circ(\underbrace{m_{0,1}^{M}\bullet_{1}(h^{M}\bullet_{1}m_{0,1}^{M})}_{=\widetilde{m}_{T,0,2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}})\circ(i^{M}\otimes i^{A}\otimes i^{A}),

which is a summand of
m_{0,2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}
. Notice also that the number of left

5.70
.

Let
A

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{A}}

6.
Kadeishvili-type theorems for bimodule Calabi–Yau algebras

In this section, using one of the main results in
[
JM25
]
,

Theorem

6.16
, we give a proof of
Theorem

E
and
its variant for minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebras (
Theorem

6.21
). For
this, we first recall the notions of universal Massey products for

A_{\infty}
-algebras and
A_{\infty}
-bimodules, as well as the companion
Hochschild–Massey cohomology theories.

6.1.
Universal Massey products for minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebras

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra. We make the following
observations:
The
A_{\infty}
-equation for
n=3
is

0={m_{2}^{A}}\left\{m_{2}^{A}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_square-HC}}}{{=}}\Sq(m_{2}^{A}),

which is simply the associativity of the binary operation,
see
Remark

5.11
. The
A_{\infty}
-equation for
n=4
is

0={m_{2}^{A}}\left\{m_{3}^{A}\right\}+{m_{3}^{A}}\left\{m_{2}^{A}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_bracket-HC}}}{{=}}[m_{2}^{A},m_{3}^{A}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Hd}}}{{=}}d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}(m_{3}^{A}),

so that
m_{3}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{3,-1}\!\left(A\right)
is a
cocyle
. Moreover, the

A_{\infty}
-equation for
n=5
is

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle={m_{2}^{A}}\left\{m_{4}^{A}\right\}+{m_{4}^{A}}\left\{m_{2}^{A}\right\}+{m_{3}^{A}}\left\{m_{3}^{A}\right\}

\displaystyle=[m_{2}^{A},m_{4}^{A}]+\Sq(m_{3}^{A})

\displaystyle=d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}(m_{4}^{A})+\Sq(m_{3}^{A}),

so that

0=\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{3}^{A}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{5,-2}\!\left(A\right).

More generally, assume that the minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra
(A,m^{A})
is

d
-sparse,
d\geq 1
. In this case the first possibly non-zero higher operation
is
m_{d+2}^{A}
, and the
A_{\infty}
-equation for
n=d+3
is

0={m_{2}^{A}}\left\{m_{d+2}^{A}\right\}+{m_{d+2}^{A}}\left\{m_{2}^{A}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_bracket-HC}}}{{=}}[m_{2}^{A},m_{d+2}^{A}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Hd}}}{{=}}d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}(m_{d+2}^{A}),

so that
m_{d+2}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\right)
is a cocycle. The next non-trivial

A_{\infty}
-equation is that for
n=2d+3
, which yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle={m_{2}^{A}}\left\{m_{2d+2}^{A}\right\}+{m_{2d+2}^{A}}\left\{m_{2}^{A}\right\}+{m_{d+2}^{A}}\left\{m_{d+2}^{A}\right\}

\displaystyle=[m_{2}^{A},m_{2d+2}^{A}]+\Sq(m_{d+2}^{A})

\displaystyle=d_{\mathrm{Hoch}}(m_{2d+2}^{A})+\Sq(m_{d+2}^{A}),

so that

0=\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{2d+3,-2d}\!\left(A\right)

These considerations suggest that the following definition is meaningful.

6.1
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra,
d\geq 1
. The

universal Massey product (=UMP) of length
d+2
is the Hochschild class

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\right).

The Gerstenhaber square of the UMP vanishes:
\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})=0
.

6.2
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra,
d\geq 1
. It
follows from the
A_{\infty}
-morphism equations that its UMP of length
d+2
is
invariant under gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphisms,
see
[
JKM22
, Proposition 4.4.4]
.

6.3
.

The UMP of length
d+2
is also considered in
[
Sei15
, Ch. 3]
. For
d=1
,
it has been investigated for example in
[
BKS04
,
Kad82
]
and plays a crucial
role in
[
Mur22
,
JKM22
]
as well. An operadic generalisation is also one of
the key concepts used in
[
JM25
]
.

6.2.
Hochschild–Massey cohomology

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra. Recall from

Definition

6.1
its UMP of length
d+2

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\right),\qquad\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})=0.

The relations for the Gerstenhaber bracket yield

[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\},[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\},x]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstehaber_square-relations}}}{{=}}[\underbrace{\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})}_{=0},x]=0,\qquad x\in\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right).

This observation serves as motivation for the following definition.

6.4
[
JKM22
, Definition 5.2.5]
.

The
Hochschild–Massey cochain complex of
(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})
is
the bigraded vector space with the components

(6.5)

\operatorname{CM}^{p,q}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{HH}^{p,q}\!\left(A\right),\qquad p\geq 2,\ q\in\mathbb{Z},

which is equipped with the bidegree
(d+1,-d)

Hochschild–Massey
differential

(6.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{HM}}\colon\operatorname{CM}^{p,q}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{CM}^{p+d+1,q-d}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)\\
x&\longmapsto[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\},x],\end{split}

except in bidegree
(d+1,-d)
where it is given by
26
26

26

Recall that, when

\chark{\mathbf{k}}\neq 2
, we have
x^{2}=0
for elements of odd total degree by the
graded commutativity of the cup product in
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
. In any case, the
Hochschild–Massey differential always squares to zero,
see
[
JKM22
, Remark 5.2.6.]
.

(6.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{HM}}\colon\operatorname{CM}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{CM}^{2d+2,-2d}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)\\
x&\longmapsto[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\},x]+x^{2}.\end{split}

The
Hochschild–Massey cohomology of
(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})
is

(6.8)

\operatorname{HM}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{CM}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)\right).

The following Kadeishvili-type theorem, which is implicit in
[
Mur22
]
in the
case
d=1
, is our main motivation for considering the Hochschild–Massey
cohomology of a sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra. Its proof relies crucially on
the obstruction theory developed by the second-named author in
[
Mur20
]
,
see also
[
JKM22
, Section 5]
. It is also worth mentioning that the
Hochschild–Massey cochain complex is embedded in an extension of a
Bousfield–Kan fringed spectral sequence introduced in
[
Mur20
]
, which
explains why its differential has a somewhat unusual expression in bidegree

(d+1,-d)
, where it is not given by a linear map.

6.9
[
JKM22
, Theorem B]
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebras,
d\geq 1
. Suppose
that

\operatorname{HM}^{p+2,-p}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)=0,\qquad p>d.

If
(A,m)
is any minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra structure with the same
underlying associative algebra structure,
m_{2}=m_{2}^{A}
, and with the same
UMP of length
d+2
,

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}}\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\right),

then there exists a gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism

(A,m^{A})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(A,m)
.

6.3.
Universal Massey products for minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra and
(M,m^{M})
a

d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule over it. Notice that the corresponding

A_{\infty}
-algebra
(A\oplus M,m^{A\ltimes M})
is also
d
-sparse and
minimal and hence it has a well-defined UMP of length
d+2
, namely the class

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes M}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\oplus M\right),

where we regard
A\oplus M
as an associative algebra with the square-zero
product
m_{2}^{A\ltimes M}
. Although it would seem natural to consider this
class as the bimodule analogue of the UMP for sparse
A_{\infty}
-algebras, in
order to obtain the correct results we must modify the ambient cohomology in
which it lives. The reason for this is that the Hochchild cohomology

\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\oplus M\right)
relates to the obstruction theory for the existence and
uniqueness of
arbitrary

A_{\infty}
-algebra structures on
(A\oplus M,m_{2}^{A\ltimes M})
, which need not satisfy the conditions in

Proposition

5.37
, and therefore need not correspond to

A_{\infty}
-bimodule structures on the graded
A
-bimodule
(M,m_{2}^{M})
.
This technical point is the
raison d’être
for considering the above
cohomology class rather in the bimodule Hochschild cohomology of the pair

(A,M)
, see
[
JM25
, Section 2]
for more details on this.

6.10
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra and
(M,m^{M})

a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule over it (recall that the sparsity
condition is vacuous for
d=1
). The
bimodule universal Massey product
(=bimodule UMP) of length
d+2
of
(M,m^{A})
is the cocycle

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes M}}\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}^{M}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right).

Similar to the algebra case, the bimodule Gerstenhaber square of the
bimodule UMP vanishes:
\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes M}}\}\!\!\})=0
.

6.11
.

We warn the reader of the following possible sources of confusion in

Definition

6.10
. Firstly, while the cochain

m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes M}=m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}^{M}\in\operatorname{C}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)

is a cocycle, neither of the cochains
m_{d+2}^{A}
and
m_{d+2}^{M}
need be
cocycles in
\operatorname{C}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)
, notwithstanding the fact that

m_{d+2}^{A}\in\operatorname{C}^{d+2,-2}\!\left(A\right)
is a cocyle. The reason for this is the
lower-triangular form of the bimodule Hochchild differential, see

eq.

4.37
. It follows that, while the difference

(m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}^{M})-(m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2})=m_{d+2}^{M}-m_{d+2}\in\operatorname{C}_{A^{e}}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(M,M\right)

is a cocycle, the cochains
m_{d+2}^{M}
and
m_{d+2}
need not be.

6.4.
Massey bimodule cohomology

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra and
(M,m^{M})
a

d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule over it. In view of
Definition

6.10
and
the discussion preceding it, the following definition is not surprising.

6.12
.

The
Massey bimodule cochain complex of
(M,m^{M})
is the bigraded vector space with the
components

(6.13)

\displaystyle\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,M\right),\qquad n\geq 1,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

which is endowed with the bidegree
(d+1,-d)

Massey bimodule differential
given, without exceptions, by

(6.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathrm{BimHM}}\colon\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right)&\longrightarrow\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{n+d+1,r-d}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right)\\
x&\longmapsto[\{\!\!\{{m^{A\ltimes M}}\}\!\!\},x].\end{split}

The
Massey bimodule cohomology of
(M,m^{M})
is

(6.15)

\operatorname{EM}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right)\coloneqq\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right)\right).

We are now ready to state the key technical result that we leverage in our proof
of
Theorem

D
. It can be regarded as an almost-formality
theorem for minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodules and should be compared with
Theorem

6.9
which
treats the case of minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebras. We remind the reader of
Remark

6.11
.

6.16
[
JM25
, Theorem 6.2.7]
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal

A_{\infty}
-algebra and
(M,m^{M})
a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule
over it,
d\geq 1
. Suppose that

\operatorname{EM}_{A^{e}}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right)=0,\qquad p>d.

If
(M,m^{A}+m)
is any minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule structure over
(A,m^{A})
with
the same underlying graded
A
-bimodule structure,
m_{2}=m_{2}^{M}
, and such
that

0=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{M}-m_{d+2}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(M,M\right),

then there exists a gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

(M,m^{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(M,m)
.

6.17
.

Continuing the discussion in
Remark

6.11
, in the context of

Theorem

6.16
, it follows from
Proposition

4.42
that the
condition

0=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{M}-m_{d+2}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(M,M\right),

is equivalent to the more natural condition

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}^{M}}\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}}\}\!\!\}\text{ in
}\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(A\,|\,M\right)

whenever the graded
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodule
\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(M,M\right)
is graded-symmetric. This is
the case when
M=A
is the diagonal
A
-bimodule since
\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)
is
graded-commutative and hence its diagonal bimodule is graded-symmetric.

6.18
.

Keeping
Theorem

6.16
in mind, the introduction of the bimodule
Hochchild–Massey cochain complex (
Definition

6.12
) is motivated by the
following considerations, the details of which can be found in our companion
article
[
JM25
]
. Firstly, associated to the
d
-sparse graded algebra
A

there is a space (=Kan complex)

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)

whose set of points is the set of minimal algebra
A_{\infty}
-structures on the
underlying graded vector space of
A
; here,
\mathbb{A}_{\infty}
is the
A_{\infty}
-operad,

\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)
is the endomorphism (graded) operad of
A
, and the above space is
the Dwyer–Kan mapping space in the model category of differential graded
(non-symmetric) operads endowed with the (transferred) projective model
structure
[
Lyu11
,
Mur11
]
. Using an appropriate cylinder object

I\mathbb{A}_{\infty}

[
Mur16
]
, it is not difficult to show that the set of connected
components

\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right))

identifies with the set of minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra structures on
A

considered up to gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism
[
Mur20
, Proposition 6.9]
.
Secondly, given a
d
-sparse graded
A
-bimodule
M
, we consider the space

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right),

whose set of points is now the set of minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra structures on

A\oplus M
that satisfy the conditions in

Proposition

5.37
; here,
\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)
is the linear
endomorphism (graded) operad of the pair
(A,M)
introduced in
[
BM09
]
.
Finally, the linear endomorphism operad is equipped with a canonical
projection
\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)
that induces a
fibration

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right)\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)

between the above mapping spaces. Given a point
m^{A}
in

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)
, we consider the induced fibre sequence

\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{\infty},m^{A}}\!\left(M\right)\rightarrowtail\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right)\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right),

where
\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{\infty},m^{A}}\!\left(M\right)
is, by construction, the space of minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule
structures on the underlying graded vector space of
M
over the given minimal

A_{\infty}
-algebra
(A,m^{A})
. From this perspective,
Theorem

6.16

provides sufficient conditions for two minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule structures

m^{A}+m^{M},m^{A}+m\in\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{\infty},m^{A}}\!\left(M\right)
to lie in the same connected
component, that is for the minimal
A_{\infty}
-bimodules
(M,m^{A}+m^{M})

and
(M,m^{A}+m)
to be gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphic. The bimodule
Hochchild–Massey cochain complex then emerges as follows. There is a
commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{\infty},m^{A}}\!\left(M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{n},m_{\leq n}^{A}}\!\left(M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{n},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{n},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{3},m_{\leq 3}^{A}}\!\left(M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{3},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{3},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{2},m_{\leq 2}^{A}}\!\left(M\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{2},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A,M\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{dgOp}}\!\left(\mathbb{A}_{2},\mathcal{E}\!\left(A\right)\right)}

where
\mathbb{A}_{n}
is the
A_{n}
-operad (whose algebras are truncated

A_{\infty}
-algebras with operations only up to and including arity
n
), whose
rows are fibre sequences based at the apparent base points induced by the
minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra structure
m^{A}
and its truncations
m_{\leq n}^{A}
, whose columns are based towers of fibrations, and whose the top row is
obtained by passing to the homotopy limit of each of the towers. Each of these
towers of fibrations gives rise to an extended Bousfield--Kan spectral
sequence
27
27

27

In the case of the rightmost column, this spectral sequence
was constructed by the second-named author for
d=1
in
[
Mur20
]
and
for
d\geq 1
by the authors in
[
Mur20
]
, see
also
[
BK72
, Sec. IX.4]
.
for computing the homotopy groups of the
homotopy limit at the top of the tower. These extended spectral sequences are
fringed: they are not fully defined in the right half-plane, and some of their
terms are possibly non-abelian groups while others are plain pointed sets. In
the case of the leftmost column, the relevant part of the
(d+1)
-st page of
the spectral sequence has the terms

E_{d+1}^{n-1,r}=\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,M\right)=\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(M,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes{M}}}\}\!\!\}\right),\qquad n\geq 2,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

and the bidegree
(d+1,-d)
differentials

\mathrm{d}_{d+1}\colon\colon E_{d+1}^{n-1,r}\longrightarrow E_{d+1}^{n+d,r-d}

is given by
x\mapsto[\{\!\!\{{m^{A\ltimes M}}\}\!\!\},x]
(that one can compute
far-away pages and differentials of the spectral sequence is a consequence of
the
d
-sparsity of
A
and
M
). The proof of
Theorem

6.16
relies
heavily on the use of the above spectral sequence, as it permits us to control
the collapse of the
\lim\nolimits^{1}
term in the Milnor short exact sequence
of pointed sets
[
BK72
, Theorem IX.3.1]

*\rightarrow\lim\nolimits^{1}\pi_{1}(\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{n},m_{\leq n}^{A}}\!\left(M\right))\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{\infty},m^{A}}\!\left(M\right))\rightarrow\varprojlim\pi_{0}(\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{n},m_{\leq n}^{A}}\!\left(M\right))\rightarrow*

Finally, notice that if the
\lim\nolimits^{1}
term collapses, then two

A_{\infty}
-bimodule structures

m^{A}+m^{M},\ m^{A}+m^{A}\in\operatorname{Str}_{\mathbb{A}_{\infty},m^{A}}\!\left(M\right)

are gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphic if and only if all of their truncations to an

A_{n}
-bimodule structure are gauge
A_{n}
-isomorphic (which is in principle a
weaker statement). This turns out to suffice for proving

Theorem

6.16
.

6.5.
A Kadeishvili-type theorem for right Calabi–Yau

A_{\infty}
-algebras

We state the following definition for the sake of completeness:

6.19
Kontsevich, see also
[
KS06
, Section 10.2]
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be an
A_{\infty}
-algebra whose cohomology (with respect to

m_{1}^{A}
) is degree-wise finite dimensional and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(1)

A
bimodule right
n
-Calabi–Yau structure
on
(A,m^{A})
is an

A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

\varphi\colon(A(n),m^{A[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(DA,m^{DA}).

(2)

We say that
(A,m^{A})
is
right
n
-Calabi–Yau as a bimodule
if
it admits a bimodule right
n
-Calabi–Yau structure.

We remind the reader of
4.65
and of the BV operator introduced
in
Definition

4.76
.

6.20
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra,
d\geq 1
, such
that the graded algebra
\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)
is unital. Suppose also that there exists an
isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules

\varphi\colon A(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DA

for some
n\in d\mathbb{Z}
. Then,

0=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A[0]}-\Upsilon(m_{d+2}^{A[0]})}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A,A\right)\cong\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A\right)

if and only if

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A\right).

Proof.

We being by recalling an important fact: By
[
LH03
, Théorème .2.1.1]
or

[
Sei08
, Lemma 2.1]
, the minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra
(A,m^{A})
is gauge

A_{\infty}
-isomorphic to a strictly unital minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra. Since
the algebra UMPs and bimodule UMPs involved are invariant under gauge

A_{\infty}
-isomorphisms, we may assume
(A,m^{A})
to be strictly unital.

In order to prove the claim in the proposition, recall from
Corollary

4.74

that there is an isomorphism of Gerstenhaber algebras

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\kappa\right)\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,A\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\![\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^{2}).

This isomorphism maps

\kappa\colon\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A[0]}-\Upsilon(m_{d+2}^{A[0]})}\}\!\!\}\longmapsto\{\!\!\{{-\kappa(\Upsilon(m_{d+2}^{A[0]}))}\}\!\!\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {rmk:BV-motivation}}}{{=}}\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}),

since

\kappa(m_{d+2}^{A[0]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:kappa}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{d+2}\pm\underbrace{m_{d+2}^{A}(\mathrm{id}_{A}^{i-1}\otimes 1_{A}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{A}^{\otimes(d+2-i)})}_{=0}=0

already at the cochain level since the minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra
(A,m^{A})

is strictly unital (
Remark

5.6
). Since
\kappa
is an
isomorphism, it follows that

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A[0]}-\Upsilon(m^{A[0]})}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A,A\right)

if and only if

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A\right),

which is what we needed to show.
∎

We are ready to prove the following result, which can be regarded as a
Kadeishvili-type theorem for bimodule right Calabi–Yau algebras.

6.21
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a
d
-sparse minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra,
d\geq 1
, such
that the graded algebra
\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)
is unital. Suppose that the Massey
bimodule cohomology of the diagonal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule satisfies

\operatorname{EM}_{A^{e}}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes A(0)}}\}\!\!\}\right)=0,\qquad p>d.

Suppose also that there exists an isomorphism of graded
A
-bimodules

\varphi\colon A(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DA

for some
n\in d\mathbb{Z}
. If

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(A\right),

then there exists an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

(A(n),m^{A[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(DA,m^{DA}),

so that
(A,m^{A})
is
n
-Calabi–Yau as a bimodule.

Proof.

Theorem

6.16
and
Proposition

6.20
yield the existence of an

A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

\varphi\colon(A(0),m^{A[0]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(A(0),\Upsilon(m^{A[0]})).

Proposition

5.52
yields further (strict)
A_{\infty}
-isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A(0),m^{A[0]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A(0),\Upsilon(m^{A[0]}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((DM)(-n),m^{(DM)(-n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(D(M(n)),m^{D(M(n))})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

and, applying
Proposition

5.47
to the resulting composite, we
would obtain an desired
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

(A(n),m^{A[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(DM,m^{DM}).\qed

6.22
.

Let
(A,m^{A})
be a strictly unital minimal
A_{\infty}
-algebra. Recall again
from
Corollary

4.74
the isomorphism of Gerstenhaber algebras

\operatorname{H}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\kappa\right)\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\,|\,A\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(A\right)\![\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^{2}).

It follows that there is an induced isomorphism of bigraded vector
spaces
28
28

28

Recall that, by definition,

\operatorname{CM}^{1,*}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right)=0

while

\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{1,*}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes A(0)}}\}\!\!\}\right)=\operatorname{Ext}_{A^{e}}^{1,*}\!\left(A,A\right).

(6.23)

\operatorname{CM}_{A^{e}}^{n,r}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes A(0)}}\}\!\!\}\right)\cong\operatorname{CM}^{n,r}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right),\qquad n\geq 2,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

where the right-hand side is the Hochschild–Massey cochain complex of the

A_{\infty}
-algebra
(A,m^{A})
, and the left-hand side is the Massey
bimodule cochain complex of the diagonal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule.
Moreover, as in the proof of
Theorem

6.21
, we notice that

\kappa(m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}^{A[0]})=m_{d+2}^{A}+\underbrace{\kappa(m_{d+2}^{A[0]})}_{=0}=m_{d+2}^{A}

by strict unitality, so that at the cohomological level the
bimodule

UMP of the diagonal
A_{\infty}
-bimodule of
A
corresponds to the
algebra
UMP
of
A
:

\kappa\colon\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}+m_{d+2}^{A[0]}}\}\!\!\}\longmapsto\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}.

It follows that the isomorphisms in (
6.23
)
are compatible with the corresponding differentials in all bidegrees with the
exception, when
\chark{\mathbf{k}}=2
, of the bidegree
(d+1,-d)
, where the
Hochschild–Massey differential has a different expression, see

eq.

6.7
. In particular, there are isomorphisms

\operatorname{EM}_{A^{e}}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes A(0)}}\}\!\!\}\right)\cong\operatorname{HM}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\right),\qquad p>d+1

From the perspective of
Theorem

6.21
, the crucial difference is then in the
Massey bimodule cohomology

\operatorname{EM}_{A^{e}}^{d+2,-d-1}\!\left(A,\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes A(0)}}\}\!\!\}\right),

which can be identified, using
\kappa
, with the cohomology of the three-term
complex

\operatorname{HH}^{1,0}\!\left(A\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d-1}\!\left(A\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{HH}^{2d+3,-2d-2}\!\left(A\right)

in which both maps are given by the formula
x\mapsto[m^{A},x]
.

6.6.
A Kadeishvili-type theorem for right Calabi–Yau dg algebras

Any possible minimal model of a dg algebra admits a direct gauge

A_{\infty}
-isomorphism to one obained as in
Theorem

5.66
. In particular, the
following definition is consistent.

6.24
.

Let
A
be a cohomologically
d
-sparse dg algebra,
d\geq 1
. The

universal Massey product (=UMP) of length
d+2
of
A
is defined as
the UMP of any of its minimal models
(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)})
:

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\}\coloneqq\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\right).

Similarly, minimal models of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules are also unique up to gauge

A_{\infty}
-isomorphism, see
[
JM25
, Remark 2.3.8]
29
29

29

This fact does not follow
immediately from corresponding statement for
A_{\infty}
-algebras since one
needs to guarantee that the gauge
A_{\infty}
-isomorphisms corresponds to an

A_{\infty}
-bimodule isomorphism, compare with

Definition

5.40
.
Thus, the following definition is also
consistent.

6.25
.

Let
A
be a cohomologically
d
-sparse dg algebra and
M
a cohomologically

d
-sparse dg
A
-bimodule,
d\geq 1
. The
universal Massey product
(=UMP) of length
d+2
of
m
is defined as the UMP of any of the minimal
models
(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)},\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)})
of the pair
(A,M)
:

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A\ltimes M}}\}\!\!\}\coloneqq\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\ltimes\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\,|\,\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)\right),

where
m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\ltimes\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}=m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}+m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(M\right)}
.

6.26
.

The notation in
Definitions

6.24
and
6.25
conflicts slightly with our
notation
m^{A}+m^{M}
for the square-zero product on
A\oplus M
. Since dg
algebras have vanishing higher operations, this abuse of
notation should not lead to confusion.

The following result shows that the vanishing condition in

Theorem

C
(
2
) is satisfied by all
bimodule right Calabi–Yau algebras with
d
-sparse cohomology.

6.27
.

Let
A
be a cohomologically
d
-sparse dg algebra,
d\geq 1
. Suppose that
A

admits a bimodule right
n
-Calabi–Yau structure

\widetilde{\varphi}\colon A[n]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DA

for some
n\in d\mathbb{Z}
, Then,

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{A}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\right),

where
\Delta=\Delta_{\varphi}
is the BV operator associated to the induced
isomorphism of graded
\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodules

\varphi\colon\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right).

Proof.

The isomorphism
\varphi
is the linear part of an
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of

A_{\infty}
-bimodules between minimal models, say

(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(n),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(n)})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(D\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),m^{D\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)})

of the dg
A
-bimodules
A[n]
and
DA
, respectively, over a compatible
minimal model of the dg algebra
A
, see for example
[
JM25
, Remark 2.3.8]
. It
then follows from
Proposition

5.52
that there exists a gauge

A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(0),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(0)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),\Upsilon(m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(0)})).

Consequently, there is an equality of bimodule UMPs of length
d+2

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}+m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(0)}}\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}+\Upsilon(m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(0)})}\}\!\!\}\\
\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\,|\,\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(0)\right).

Forming their difference and applying
Proposition

6.20
yields the
vanishing condition

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\right),

where
\Delta=\Delta_{\varphi}
, which is what we needed to prove.
∎

6.7.
The proof of
Theorem

E

We are ready to prove
Theorem

E
, which is a translation of

Theorem

6.21
to the language of dg algebras and dg bimodules. It
is also a partial converse to
Proposition

6.27
.

Proof of
Theorem

E
.

Let
(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)})
be a minimal model for
A
; in view of th
discussion in
Example

5.70
, the corresponding diagonal

A_{\infty}
-bimodule is indeed a minimal model for the diagonal dg

A
-bimodule. Similarly, minimal models of the dg
A
-bimodules
A[n]
and

DA
are obtained by shifting and dualising the minimal model of the diagonal
dg
A
-bimodule, respectively.
Theorem

6.21
then yields an

A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-bimodules

(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(n),m^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)(n)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(D\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),m^{D\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}),

the existence of which implies the existence of the desired quasi-isomorphism
of dg
A
-bimodules
A[n]\simeq DA
by
[
JM25
, Proposition 2.4.6]
, which
says that quasi-isomorphic DG bimodules can be distinguished by their minimal models.
∎

7.
The proofs of

Theorems

C
and
D

7.1.
The
d
-sparse graded algebra
\mathbf{\Lambda}

7.1
.

We fix integers
d\geq 1
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and set
n\coloneq dm
. We also fix a
basic Frobenius algebra
\Lambda
, a (
\mathbf{k}
-linear) algebra automorphism

\sigma\in\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda)
, and introduce the graded algebra

\mathbf{\Lambda}\coloneqq\frac{\Lambda\langle\imath^{\pm 1}\rangle}{(\imath x-\sigma(x)\imath)_{x\in\Lambda}},\qquad|\imath|=-d.

As suggested by the notation, the graded algebra
\mathbf{\Lambda}
is not
graded-commutative unless
d
is even.

7.2
.

The graded algebra
\mathbf{\Lambda}
is
d
-sparse in the sense that it is
concentrated in degrees that are multiples of
d
. Indeed, the possibly
non-zero homogeneous components of
\mathbf{\Lambda}
are given by

\mathbf{\Lambda}^{dk}=\imath^{-k}\Lambda=\left\{\imath^{-k}x\,\middle|\,x\in\Lambda\right\},\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z}.

Moreover, by definition, for
x\in\Lambda
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
there are equalities

(7.3)

\imath^{k}x\imath^{-k}=\sigma^{k}(x)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\imath^{-k}x\imath^{k}=\sigma^{-k}(x).

In particular, the map

(7.4)

\mathbf{\Lambda}^{dk}=\imath^{-k}\Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{}_{\sigma^{k}}{\Lambda}_{1},\qquad\imath^{-k}x\longmapsto x,

is an isomorphism of
\Lambda
-bimodules. These isomorphisms assemble into a
further isomorphism of graded algebras

\mathbf{\Lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda(\sigma,d)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{dk\in d\mathbb{Z}}{}_{\sigma^{k}}{\Lambda}_{1},

where the algebra structure on the right-hand side is given by

x*y=\sigma^{k}(x)y,\qquad|y|=dk.

We prefer to work with the graded algebra
\mathbf{\Lambda}
in what follows since
there is no ambiguity on the degrees of its homogeneous elements.

7.5
.

The choice of algebra automorphism

\sigma\in\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda)
uniquely determines an action of the cyclic group

\mathbb{Z}
on
\Lambda
. If we identify the Laurent polynomial algebra

\mathbf{k}[\imath^{\pm 1}]
with the group algebra
\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{Z}]
, we see that

\mathbf{\Lambda}
is precisely the graded skew-group algebra

\mathbf{\Lambda}=\Lambda*\mathbf{k}[\imath^{\pm 1}]=\Lambda*\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{Z}],\qquad|\imath|=-d.

In particular, if
\sigma=\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda}
then
\mathbf{\Lambda}=\Lambda\otimes\mathbf{k}[\imath^{\pm 1}]
.

The following result provides a criterion for the existence, for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
of an isomorphism of graded
\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodules

\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}DA

in terms of the Frobenius algebra
\Lambda=\mathbf{\Lambda}^{0}
.

7.6
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. There is a canonical bijection between the following:

(1)

Isomorphisms of graded

\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodules

\varphi\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D\mathbf{\Lambda}.

(2)

Associative non-degenerate bilinear pairings

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{k}

such that

(7.7)

\displaystyle\langle y,x\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\sigma^{m}(x),y\rangle

\displaystyle x,y

\displaystyle\in\Lambda,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\langle\sigma^{k}(x),y\rangle

\displaystyle=(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}\langle x,\sigma^{-k}(y)\rangle

\displaystyle x,y

\displaystyle\in\Lambda,\ k\in\mathbb{Z}.

In particular, when either of the above exist,
\sigma^{m}
is a Nakayama
automorphism of the Frobenius algebra
\Lambda
, that is

{}_{\sigma^{m}}{\Lambda}_{1}\cong D\Lambda
as
\Lambda
-bimodules. The
correspondence (
1
)
\to
(
2
)
associates to an isomorphism
\varphi
the bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(x,y)\longmapsto\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x)(y).

Proof.

We make the following observations that are helpful for keeping track of
degrees and Koszul signs throughout the proof. Recall that
n=dm
.

•

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)^{dk}=\mathbf{\Lambda}^{dk+dm}=\imath^{-(k+m)}\Lambda.

•

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x\in\Lambda
we have

\mathbf{s}^{n}\left(\imath^{-k}x\right)=\mathbf{s}^{n}\left(\imath^{-k}\right)x

in
\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)
and therefore we may write
\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-k}x
without
ambiguity. Furthermore,

|\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-k}x|=dk-dm=d(k-m).

In particular,
|\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x|=0
.

•

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x,y\in\Lambda
we have

x\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-k}y=\mathbf{s}^{n}x\imath^{-k}y

since
x
is homogeneous of degree
0
(as an element of
\bf\Lambda
).

Let
{\varphi\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D\mathbf{\Lambda}}
be an
isomorphism of graded
\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodules and consider the induced
non-degenerate associative bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle_{\varphi}\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\times\mathbf{\Lambda}\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(s^{n}\imath^{-k}x,\imath^{-\ell}y)\longmapsto\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}1)(\imath^{-k}x\imath^{-\ell}y);

recall from
Corollary

4.63
that this pairing is
graded-symmetric with respect to the degree in
\mathbf{\Lambda}
, that is

(7.9)

\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-k}x,\imath^{-\ell}y\rangle_{\varphi}=(-1)^{dkd\ell}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-\ell}y,\imath^{-k}x\rangle,\qquad x,y\in\Lambda,\ k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}.

Since the restriction

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\imath^{-m}\Lambda=\mathbf{\Lambda}^{n}=\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)^{0}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(D\mathbf{\Lambda})^{0}=D\Lambda

\displaystyle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x

\displaystyle\longmapsto(y\mapsto\varphi(\imath^{-m}x)(y))

to the corresponding components degree
0
is an isomorphism of

\Lambda
-bimodules, the induced bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(x,y)\longmapsto\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x,y\rangle_{\varphi}

is non-degenerate and associative. Therefore, for
x,y\in\Lambda
we have

\displaystyle\langle y,x\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}y,x\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:graded-symmetric-pairing-lambda}}}{{=}}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x,\imath^{-m}y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle|x|

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}x\imath^{-m},y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:imath-conjugation}}}{{=}}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}\sigma^{m}(x),y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\langle\sigma^{m}(x),y\rangle.

Similarly, for
x,y\in\Lambda
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\langle\sigma^{k}(x),y\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}\sigma^{k}(x),y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:imath-conjugation}}}{{=}}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}\imath^{k}x\imath^{-k},y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{k},\imath^{-m}x\imath^{-k}y\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:graded-symmetric-pairing-lambda}}}{{=}}(-1)^{dk(dm+dk)}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x\imath^{-k}y,\imath^{k}\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle|\imath^{-m}|=dm,\ |\imath^{-k}|=dk

\displaystyle=(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x,\imath^{-k}y\imath^{k}\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:imath-conjugation}}}{{=}}(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x,\sigma^{-k}(y)\rangle_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}\langle x,\sigma^{-k}(y)\rangle.

This shows that the association

\varphi\longmapsto\langle-,-\rangle_{\varphi}|_{\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)^{0}\times\mathbf{\Lambda}^{0}}

yields a well-defined map
(
1
)
\mapsto
(
2
).

Conversely, suppose given an associative non-degenerate pairing

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{k}

with the additional properties listed in statement (
2
) of the
proposition. We claim that the isomorphism of graded vector spaces

\varphi=\varphi_{\langle-,-\rangle}\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D\mathbf{\Lambda}

with the components

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)^{dk}=\imath^{-(k+m)}\Lambda

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(\imath^{k}\Lambda)=(D\mathbf{\Lambda})^{dk}

\displaystyle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-(k+m)}x

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\imath^{k}y\mapsto\langle\sigma^{-k}(x),y\rangle)

is in fact an isomorphism of graded
\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodules. Indeed, let

w,x,y,z\in\Lambda
and
r,s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
; for readability we set
u\coloneqq r+s+t
. On the one hand,

\displaystyle\varphi(\imath^{-r}x\cdot\mathbf{s}^{n}\left(\imath^{-(s+m)}y\right)\cdot\imath^{-t}z)(\imath^{u}w)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{drdm}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-(u+m)}\sigma^{s+t+m}(x)\sigma^{t}(y)z)(\imath^{u}w)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{drdm}\langle\sigma^{m-r}(x)\sigma^{-r-s}(y)\sigma^{-u}(z),w\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:props-pairing-Nakayama}}}{{=}}(-1)^{drdm}\langle\sigma^{-r-s}(y)\sigma^{-u}(z),w\sigma^{-r}(x)\rangle

\displaystyle=(-1)^{drdm}\langle\sigma^{-r-s}(y),\sigma^{-u}(z)w\sigma^{-r}(x)\rangle.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle(\imath^{-r}x\cdot\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-(s+m)}y)\cdot\imath^{-t}z)(\imath^{u}w)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{dr(ds+dt+du)}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-(s+m)}y)(\imath^{-t}z\imath^{u}w\imath^{-r}x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{dr}\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-(s+m)}y)(\imath^{s}\sigma^{r-u}(z)\sigma^{r}(w)x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{dr}\langle\sigma^{-s}(y),\sigma^{r-u}(z)\sigma^{r}(w)x\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:props-pairing-shift}}}{{=}}(-1)^{dr}(-1)^{dr(dm+1)}\langle\sigma^{-r-s}(y),\sigma^{-u}(z)w\sigma^{-r}(x)\rangle

\displaystyle=(-1)^{drdm}\langle\sigma^{-r-s}(y),\sigma^{-u}(z)w\sigma^{-r}(x)\rangle.

This shows that the construction

\langle-,-\rangle\longmapsto\varphi_{\langle-,-\rangle}

yields a well defined map
(
2
)
\mapsto
(
1
).

We now verify that the maps
(
1
)
\mapsto
(
2
) and
(
2
)
\mapsto
(
1
) defined above are
inverse to each other. Indeed, suppose given a bimodule isomorphism
\varphi

as in (
1
) and write

\langle-,-\rangle=\langle-,-\rangle_{\varphi}|_{\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)^{0}\times\mathbf{\Lambda}^{0}}
.
Then, for
x,y\in\Lambda
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\langle-,-\rangle}(\imath^{-(k+m)}x)(\imath^{k}y)

\displaystyle=\langle\sigma^{-k}(x),y\rangle

\displaystyle=\varphi(\imath^{-m}\sigma^{-k}(x))(y)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:imath-conjugation}}}{{=}}\varphi(\imath^{-m}\imath^{-k}x\imath^{k})(y)

\displaystyle=\varphi(\imath^{-(k+m)}x)(\imath^{k}y).

Similarly, suppose given a paring
\langle-,-\rangle
as in (
2
) and
write
\varphi=\varphi_{\langle-,-\rangle}
. Then, for
x,y\in\Lambda
,

\langle\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x,y\rangle_{\varphi}=\varphi(\mathbf{s}^{n}\imath^{-m}x)(y)=\langle x,y\rangle.

This finishes the proof.
∎

7.10
.

Suppose given a pairing

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{k}

as in
Proposition

7.6
(
2
). Then,
according to (
7.7
) we must have

\langle\sigma^{m}(x),y\rangle=\langle y,x\rangle=\langle x,\sigma^{-m}(y)\rangle\qquad x,y\in\Lambda.

On the other hand, according to (
7.8
) for
k=m
we also must
have

\langle\sigma^{m}(x),y\rangle=(-1)^{dm(dm+1)}\langle x,\sigma^{-m}(y)\rangle\qquad x,y\in\Lambda.

Notice, however, that
dm(dm+1)
is always even.

7.11
.

Let
\Lambda=\mathbf{k}
and
\sigma=\mathrm{id}
, the unique
\mathbf{k}
-linear algebra automorphism of

\mathbf{k}
. Every non-degenerate bilinear pairing
\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}\to\mathbf{k}
is
necessarily of the form

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}\longrightarrow\mathbf{k},\qquad(x,y)\longmapsto uxy,

where
u\in\mathbf{k}
is non-zero. In particular, any such pairing is associative
and satisfies (
7.7
) since
\mathbf{k}
is commutative.
However, condition (
7.7
) is satisfied if and only
if either of the following conditions hold:

•

\chark(\mathbf{k})=2
.

•

d
is even.

•

n=dm
is odd.

Indeed, for condition (
7.7
) to hold for the above
pairing it is necessary and sufficient to have

(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}=1\in\mathbf{k}\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z}.

We conclude from
Proposition

7.6
that there exists
an isomorphism of graded
\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodules

\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad n\coloneqq dm,

if and only if either of the three conditions listed above are satisfied.
Notice that
\mathbf{\Lambda}=\mathbf{k}[\imath^{\pm 1}]
with
|\imath|=-d
in this case.

7.12
.

Let
\Lambda=\mathbf{k}[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^{2})
be the algebra of dual numbers.
The algebra
\Lambda
is symmetric since it is commutative and self-injective, see
for example
[
SY11
, Proposition IV.4.6]
. The vector space

\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda^{e}}\!\left(\Lambda,D\Lambda\right)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda^{e}}\!\left(\Lambda,\Lambda\right)\cong Z(\Lambda)=\Lambda

is therefore
2
-dimensional: To a pair
a,b\in\mathbf{k}

we associate the morphism
\Lambda
-bimodules

\varphi=\varphi_{a,b}\colon\Lambda\longrightarrow D\Lambda

that is adjoint to the associative and symmetric bilinear pairing

\langle-,-\rangle=\langle-,-\rangle_{a,b}\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbf{k}

uniquely determined by the requirement that

\displaystyle\langle 1,1\rangle

\displaystyle=a

\displaystyle\langle 1,\varepsilon\rangle

\displaystyle=b

\displaystyle\langle\varepsilon,1\rangle

\displaystyle=b

\displaystyle\langle\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle

\displaystyle=0;

explicitly,

\varphi(1)=a1^{*}+b\varepsilon^{*}\qquad{and}\qquad\varphi(\varepsilon)=b1^{*}.

By the determinant criterion, such a pairing is non-degenerate if and only if

b\neq 0
, in which case the inverse of
\varphi=\varphi_{a,b}
is the map

\displaystyle\psi=\psi_{a,b}\colon D\Lambda

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Lambda,

\displaystyle 1^{*}

\displaystyle\longmapsto b^{-1}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{*}

\displaystyle\longmapsto b^{-1}(1+ab^{-1})\varepsilon.

Fix now
a,b\in\mathbf{k}
with
b\neq 0
, and hence a
\Lambda
-bimodule isomorphism

\varphi=\varphi_{a,b}
as above. Fix
d\geq 1
and consider
the algebra automorphism of
\Lambda
that is uniquely determined by the requirement that
\sigma(\varepsilon)=(-1)^{d}\varepsilon
(compare with
[
JKM22
, Corollary 5.4.12]
).
Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and set
n\coloneqq dm
. We analyse under which
conditions are equalities (
7.7
) and
(
7.8
) in
Proposition

7.6
satisfied. This is clearly the case if

\chark(\mathbf{k})=2
, so let us assume that
\chark(\mathbf{k})\neq 2
. In this case

\displaystyle\langle 1,\varepsilon\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}\langle\sigma^{m}(\varepsilon),1\rangle=(-1)^{dm}\langle\varepsilon,1\rangle=(-1)^{dm}\langle 1,\varepsilon\rangle

is satisfied if and only if
d
or
m
are even
(recall that
\langle 1,\varepsilon\rangle\neq 0
). Similarly, for the equality

\displaystyle(-1)^{dk}\langle\varepsilon,1\rangle=\langle\sigma^{k}(\varepsilon),1\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}\langle\varepsilon,\sigma^{-k}(1)\rangle=(-1)^{dk(dm+1)}\langle\varepsilon,1\rangle

to hold for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
in addition to the previous one requires that either

d
is even and
m
is arbitrary or that

d
is odd and
m
is even (hence in both cases
n=md
is even).

7.2.
Proofs of
Theorems

C
and
D

We are finally ready to give our proof of

Theorems

C
and
D
.

Proof of
Theorem

D
.

We show first that the correspondence

\eqref{it:thm:CY-correspondence:dgAs}\to\eqref{it:thm:CY-correspondence:gLambda}

in
Theorem

D
is well defined. It is part
of
[
JKM22
, Theorem A]
that the correspondence is well defined if we drop
items (
1c
) and
(
2c
) in

Theorem

D
; it is here that we need the assumption that the
ground field is perfect, which is also needed in
loc. cit.
Suppose then
that
A
is a dg algebra as in

Theorem

D
(
1
) which
therefore satisfies conditions (
1a
) and
(
1b
) therein, and hence the pair

(\Lambda,I)\coloneqq(\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right),\operatorname{H}^{-d}\!\left(A\right))

satisfies the conditions in (
2a
) and
(
2b
) in
Theorem

D
.
Moreover, there is an isomorphism of graded algebras, which we treat as an
identification in what follows,

\mathbf{\Lambda}=\Lambda(\sigma,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right),\qquad\imath\longmapsto u,

that restricts to the identity on the degree
0
part and it is otherwise
determined by the choice of an isomorphism

u\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}^{\mathrm{c}}\!\left(A\right)}\!\left(A,A[-d]\right)\cong\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)[-d],

in the derived category of (right) dg
A
-modules (the latter exists since
A

satisfies conditions (
1a
) and
(
1b
) in

Theorem

D
, see
[
JKM22
, Section 4.5.1]
for details).
Since the dg algebra
A
is bimodule right
n
-Calabi–Yau by condition

Theorem

D
(
1c
), we
may apply
Proposition

6.27
to obtain the vanishing condition

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\right),

where
\Delta=\Delta_{\varphi}
is the BV operator associated to
\varphi
.
Notice that the conditions on the class
\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)}}\}\!\!\}
listed in

Theorem

D
(
2c
)
uniquely characterise the UMP of
A
up to
A_{\infty}
-isomorphism of its
minimal models, see the proof of
[
JKM22
, Theorem 5.3.3]
. Finally, it
follows from
[
JKM22
, Theorem 5.1.2]
that the class of any short exact
sequence of
\Lambda
-bimodules

\eta\colon\quad 0\to I\to P_{d+2}\to\cdots\to P_{3}\to P_{2}\to P_{1}\to\Lambda\to 0

with projective(-injective) middle terms can be realised as the restricted UMP
of a minimal model for a dg algebra in the quasi-isomorphism class of
A
, hence the previous argument shows that the
vanishing condition in (
2c
) indeed
holds for arbitrary
\eta
. This finishes the proof of the fact that the
correspondence

\eqref{it:thm:CY-correspondence:dgAs}\to\eqref{it:thm:CY-correspondence:gLambda}

is well defined.

We now conclude the proof of the theorem by proving that the correspondence

\eqref{it:thm:CY-correspondence:dgAs}\to\eqref{it:thm:CY-correspondence:gLambda}

is surjective; as explained in the introduction, the correspondence is
injective since it is the restriction of the bijective correspondence
in
[
JKM22
, Theorem A]
. Let
\Lambda
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}
as in

Theorem

D
(
2
). In
particular, there exists an isomorphism of graded
\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodules

\varphi\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D\mathbf{\Lambda}.

Assuming that the conditions (
2a
)
and (
2b
) in

Theorem

D
hold,

[
JKM22
, Theorems 5.1.2 and 5.3.3]
guarantee the existence of a minimal

A_{\infty}
-algebra structure
(\mathbf{\Lambda},m^{\mathbf{\Lambda}})
with the property
that

\displaystyle j^{*}\colon\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{HH}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right),\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle\longmapsto j^{*}\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{{\eta}\}\!\!\},

where

\eta\colon\quad 0\to{}_{1}{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\to P_{d+2}\to\cdots P_{3}\to P_{2}\to P_{1}\to\Lambda\to 0

is any given short exact sequence of
\Lambda
-bimodules with
projective(-injective) middle terms. Since UMPs of length
d+2
always satisfy

\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\})=0
, by assumption we must also have

0=\Delta(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\})\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+1,-d}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right),

where
\Delta=\Delta_{\varphi}
is the BV operator. Now, as the graded algebra

\mathbf{\Lambda}
is
d
-sparse by construction, we find ourselves in the setting of

Theorem

E
and hence we are able to conclude that the dg
algebra
A
is right
n
-Calabi–Yau as a bimodule as soon as we establish the
following vanishing of the Massey bimodule cohomology of the diagonal

\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodule:

\operatorname{EM}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}^{e}}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}(0),\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}\ltimes\mathbf{\Lambda}(0)}}\}\!\!\}\right),\qquad p>d.

We finish the proof of the theorem by establishing this vanishing. Keeping in
mind the discussion in
Remark

6.22
, in what follows we identify the
Massey bimodule cochain complex of the diagonal

\mathbf{\Lambda}
-bimodule with the bigraded vector space with the components

\operatorname{HH}^{p,r}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right),\qquad p\geq 1,\ r\in\mathbb{Z},

that is endowed with the bidegree
(d+1,-d)
differential

x\mapsto[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]
, where

\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}
is the (algebra) UMP of length
d+2
. From
this point onwards the proof is very similarly (in fact slightly simpler) to
the proof of
[
JKM22
, Proposition 5.2.9]
, which is a key result in

op. cit.
Consider the bidegree
(d+2,-d)
endomorphism of

\operatorname{CM}^{\bullet,*}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda},\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}\right)
given by

(7.13)

\operatorname{HH}^{s,t}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{HH}^{s+d+2,t-d}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right),\qquad x\longmapsto{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x};

unlike in the proof of
[
JKM22
, Proposition 5.2.9]
where the algebra
Hochchild–Massey cohomology is considered, there is no need to change the
definition in bidegree
(d+1,-d)
because the Massey bimodule
differential is given by the formula
{x\mapsto[m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}},x]}

without exceptions, see
Definition

6.10
. The same calculation as in

[
JKM22
, Proposition 5.2.9]
shows that the above maps indeed determine an
cochain endomorphism, which is to say that

[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x}]={\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]}.

We reproduce the proof of the latter equality for the convenience of the
reader:

\displaystyle[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_relation}}}{{=}}{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}]}\cdot{x}+{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_bracket-HC}}}{{=}}{2\underbrace{\Sq(\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\})}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {def:UMP}}}{{=}}0}}\cdot{x}+{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]}

\displaystyle={\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]}.

In bidegree
(d+2,-d-1)
, the ‘lowest’ relevant bidegree from the perspective
of the vanishing conditions required to apply
Theorem

6.21
, the situation
looks as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{1,0}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d-1}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{2d+3,-2d-2}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{d+3,-d}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{2d+4,-2d-1}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{HH}^{3d+5,-3d-2}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{?}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{?}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{?}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{?}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{?}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},-]}

We also know from
[
JKM22
, Proposition 4.7.15]
that the maps
(
7.13
) are isomorphisms in source bidegrees
(s,t)
,
s\geq 2
, and
hence, in particular, they induce cohomology-level isomorphisms

(7.14)

\operatorname{EM}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}^{e}}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}(0),\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}\ltimes{\mathbf{\Lambda}(0)}}}\}\!\!\}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{EM}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}^{e}}^{p+d+3,-p-d}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}(0),\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}\ltimes{\mathbf{\Lambda}(0)}}}\}\!\!\}\right),\quad p>d.

Still as in the proof of
[
JKM22
, Proposition 5.2.9]
, the map
(
7.13
) is null-homotopic via the bidegree
(1,0)
maps

\operatorname{HH}^{s,r}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)\longrightarrow\operatorname{HH}^{s+1,t}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right),\qquad x\longmapsto{\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x},

where
\delta\in\operatorname{C}^{1,0}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}\right)
is the fractional Euler derivation

\delta_{/d}\colon\mathbf{\Lambda}\longrightarrow\mathbf{\Lambda},\qquad x\longmapsto\tfrac{|x|}{d}x,

where we use that
|x|\in d\mathbb{Z}
since the graded algebra
\mathbf{\Lambda}
is

d
-sparse by assumption (we refer the reader to
[
JKM22
, Section 4.7]
for
more information on this derivation). To prove this, we follow the
computations in
[
Mur22
, Section 3]
and observe first that

\displaystyle[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:shifted_Lie_algebra}}}{{=}}-[\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\},\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}]=\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},

where in the first equality we use that the shifted total degree
|\mathbf{s}\delta_{/d}|=0
and in the second equality we
use
[
JKM22
, Proposition 4.7.4.]
for the vertical degree
-d
of

m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}
. Combining this observation with the Gerstenhaber
relation (keeping in mind that
|\mathbf{s}m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}|=1
and

|\delta_{/d}|=1
) we obtain the identity

\displaystyle{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x}

\displaystyle=[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},{\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Gerstenhaber_relation}}}{{=}}{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}]}\cdot{x}-{\delta_{/d}}\cdot{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]},

which we may rewrite as the desired null-homotopy equation

{\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x}=[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},{\{\!\!\{{\delta_{/d}}\}\!\!\}}\cdot{x}]+{\delta_{/d}}\cdot{[\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\},x]}.

Finally, since the maps (
7.13
) are null-homotopic, we conclude that
the induced isomorphisms (
7.14
) are null maps, and hence we
must have

\operatorname{EM}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}^{e}}^{p+1,-p}\!\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}(0),\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}\ltimes{\mathbf{\Lambda}(0)}}}\}\!\!\}\right)=0,\qquad p>d,

which is what we needed to prove. This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We conclude by proving
Theorem

C
.

Proof of
Theorem

C
.

The implication
(
1
)
\Rightarrow
(
2
)
is a special case of
Proposition

6.27
, and only needs the dg algebra

A
to be cohomologically
d
-sparse and bimodule right
n
-Calabi–Yau,

n=md
. To prove the converse implication, we recall that, under the
assumptions of
Theorem

C
, the restricted UMP of length
d+2
,

j^{*}\{\!\!\{{m_{d+2}^{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\}\!\!\}\in\operatorname{HH}^{d+2,-d}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right),\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}\!\left(A\right)\right)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right)^{e}}^{\bullet}\!\left(\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right),\operatorname{H}^{-d}\!\left(A\right)\right),

can be represented by an exact sequence of
\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right)
-bimodules with
projective(-injective) middle terms
[
JKM22
, Corollary 4.5.17]
.

Theorem

D
applied to the pair
(\operatorname{H}^{0}\!\left(A\right),\operatorname{H}^{-d}\!\left(A\right))
yields
the fact that the dg algebra
A
is bimodule
n
-Calabi–Yau (here we use
again that any exact sequence
\eta
as

Theorem

D
(
2c
) can
be realised as a restricted UMP for a dg algebra in the quasi-isomorphism
class of
A

[
JKM22
, Theorem 5.1.2]
). This finishes
the proof of the theorem.
∎
